- [ ] **Step 4: Filter by current price and add one store's stock (`app/services/products.py`)**

Replace everything from the imports down to the end of `get_product` with the code below. `create_product`, `update_product`, `replace_specs` and `_specs` stay as they are.

```python
"""Catalogue: products and their specs."""
from psycopg2 import sql

from app.auth import require_catalogue_writer
from app.errors import ApiError, NotFound
from app.services import promotions
from app.services.filters import product_conditions, where
from app.services.reference import store_exists

# The columns of ProductSummary. The price comes from the view, so it includes any running promotion.
# {store_quantity} is one of the two fragments below: null when no store was asked about, otherwise that
# store's quantity (0 when the product has no inventory row there).
SUMMARY_SELECT = sql.SQL(
    """
    SELECT p.product_id, p.sku, p.name, p.brand_id, b.name AS brand, p.category_id, c.name AS category,
           p.base_price, cp.current_price, cp.promotion_id IS NOT NULL AS on_sale, p.is_active,
           COALESCE((SELECT sum(i.quantity) FROM inventory i WHERE i.product_id = p.product_id), 0)
               AS total_stock,
           {store_quantity} AS store_quantity
    FROM product p
    JOIN brand b                    ON b.brand_id = p.brand_id
    JOIN category c                 ON c.category_id = p.category_id
    JOIN v_product_current_price cp ON cp.product_id = p.product_id
    """
)
NO_STORE_QUANTITY = sql.SQL("NULL::int")
ONE_STORE_QUANTITY = sql.SQL(
    "COALESCE((SELECT i.quantity FROM inventory i WHERE i.product_id = p.product_id AND i.store_id = %s), 0)"
)

# The columns a PATCH may change. Only these names can reach the dynamic SET clause below.
UPDATABLE_COLUMNS = ("name", "brand_id", "category_id", "base_price", "is_active")


def list_products(cur, q=None, category_id=None, brand_id=None, include_inactive=False,
                  min_price=None, max_price=None, store_id=None):
    """The catalogue, filtered (R1). Prices are compared with the price a product sells for now, promotion
    included (R2), and both bounds count. A store_id adds that store's quantity to each product (R3); it
    doesn't filter the list."""
    if min_price is not None and max_price is not None and min_price > max_price:
        raise ApiError(422, "invalid_price_range", "The lowest price is above the highest price.",
                       {"min_price": str(min_price), "max_price": str(max_price)})
    if store_id is not None and not store_exists(cur, store_id):
        raise NotFound("Store", store_id)

    conditions, params = product_conditions(q, category_id, brand_id)
    if not include_inactive:
        conditions.append(sql.SQL("p.is_active"))
    if min_price is not None:
        conditions.append(sql.SQL("cp.current_price >= %s"))
        params.append(min_price)
    if max_price is not None:
        conditions.append(sql.SQL("cp.current_price <= %s"))
        params.append(max_price)

    if store_id is None:
        select = SUMMARY_SELECT.format(store_quantity=NO_STORE_QUANTITY)
    else:
        # The store's %s is in the SELECT list, ahead of every condition's, so its value goes first.
        select = SUMMARY_SELECT.format(store_quantity=ONE_STORE_QUANTITY)
        params = [store_id, *params]
    cur.execute(select + where(conditions) + sql.SQL(" ORDER BY c.name, b.name, p.name"), params)
    return cur.fetchall()


def get_product(cur, product_id: int):
    cur.execute(SUMMARY_SELECT.format(store_quantity=NO_STORE_QUANTITY) + sql.SQL("WHERE p.product_id = %s"),
                (product_id,))
    product = cur.fetchone()
    if product is None:
        raise NotFound("Product", product_id)
    product["specs"] = _specs(cur, product_id)
    cur.execute(
        """
        SELECT s.store_id, s.name AS store_name, s.store_type, s.state, COALESCE(i.quantity, 0) AS quantity
        FROM store s
        LEFT JOIN inventory i ON i.store_id = s.store_id AND i.product_id = %s
        ORDER BY s.store_id
        """,
        (product_id,),
    )
    product["stock"] = cur.fetchall()
    product["promotions"] = promotions.list_promotions(cur, product_id=product_id)
    return product
```

Why it is written this way:
- The bounds compare `cp.current_price` (R2: the price it sells for now), not `p.base_price`, so a product on sale is found at its sale price. Both bounds are inclusive.
- `psycopg2.sql` only combines fixed fragments written in this file (the `{store_quantity}` slot, the conditions). Every value, including `store_id`, is still a `%s` parameter. Because the store's `%s` sits in the SELECT list, it comes before the WHERE clause's parameters in the final text, so its value goes first.
- The store quantity is a scalar subquery with `COALESCE(…, 0)`, like `total_stock` above it. A product the store never stocked is still listed, with 0: stock is information, and delivery can ship from elsewhere.
- The range check is part of reading the request (no data involved), so it runs before the store lookup, and it lives in the service so the router stays thin. An unknown store is 404 at once: the endpoint is public, so no permission check comes first (as `GET /stores/{id}/inventory`).

- [ ] **Step 5: Take the three query parameters (`app/routers/products.py`)**

Change the imports and `list_products`; the other routes stay as they are. `Money | None = Query(None, ge=0)` follows the `Query(…, ge=0)` style of `app/routers/inventory.py`. Checked on FastAPI 0.141.1: `-1`, `cheap`, `1.005` and 11 digits are all 422 `validation_error`.

```python
from fastapi import APIRouter, Query

from app.auth import CurrentActor
from app.db import DbCursor
from app.errors import ERROR_RESPONSES
from app.schemas import Money
from app.schemas.products import ProductCreate, ProductDetail, ProductSummary, ProductUpdate, Spec
from app.services import products

router = APIRouter(prefix="/products", tags=["products"], responses=ERROR_RESPONSES)


@router.get("", response_model=list[ProductSummary])
def list_products(
    cur: DbCursor,
    q: str | None = None,
    category_id: int | None = None,
    brand_id: int | None = None,
    include_inactive: bool = False,
    min_price: Money | None = Query(None, ge=0),
    max_price: Money | None = Query(None, ge=0),
    store_id: int | None = None,
):
    return products.list_products(cur, q, category_id, brand_id, include_inactive, min_price, max_price, store_id)
```

(Task 2's `br17_order_address_active` is only named in a docstring.)

Errors: 401 `not_signed_in`; 403 `customer_only` / `not_your_account`; 404 `not_found` (PATCH, owner only); 409 `au_state_valid` / `au_postcode_format`; 422 `validation_error`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Create `tests/test_addresses.py`:

```python
"""Delivery addresses (BR16): a customer adds new ones and retires old ones — an address is never edited,
because orders keep pointing at the address they were placed with. Only the customer changes them.

These tests commit, so each one signs up a new customer (a new email) and works on that customer only.
"""
import uuid

from fastapi.testclient import TestClient

from app.auth import Actor
from app.main import app
from app.schemas.customers import AddressCreate
from app.services.customers import add_address
from tests.auth_support import as_customer, as_staff
from tests.support import connect, run_at_once

client = TestClient(app)
API = "/api/v1"
AIDEN, NORA = 14, 40            # store staff at Sydney CBD; support
NEW_ADDRESS = {"line1": "5 New Street", "suburb": "Newtown", "state": "NSW", "postcode": "2042"}
RETIRE = {"is_active": False}


def error_of(response):
    return response.status_code, response.json()["error"]["code"]


def new_customer() -> tuple[int, dict]:
    """Someone who has just signed up with a new email: (customer_id, sign-in headers). No addresses yet."""
    headers = as_customer(f"addresses.{uuid.uuid4().hex[:10]}@example.com")
    response = client.get(f"{API}/me", headers=headers)
    assert response.status_code == 200, response.text
    return response.json()["id"], headers


def add(customer_id: int, headers: dict, body=None):
    return client.post(f"{API}/customers/{customer_id}/addresses", headers=headers, json=body or NEW_ADDRESS)


def addresses_of(customer_id: int, headers: dict) -> list[tuple[int, bool]]:
    """(address_no, is_active) of each of the customer's addresses, as GET /customers/{id} lists them."""
    response = client.get(f"{API}/customers/{customer_id}", headers=headers)
    assert response.status_code == 200, response.text
    return [(address["address_no"], address["is_active"]) for address in response.json()["addresses"]]


def test_each_new_address_takes_the_next_number():
    customer_id, headers = new_customer()
    responses = [add(customer_id, headers) for _ in range(3)]
    assert [response.status_code for response in responses] == [201, 201, 201], responses[-1].text
    assert [response.json()["address_no"] for response in responses] == [1, 2, 3]
    assert responses[-1].json() == {"address_no": 3, **NEW_ADDRESS, "is_active": True}
    assert addresses_of(customer_id, headers) == [(1, True), (2, True), (3, True)]


def test_an_address_is_trimmed():
    customer_id, headers = new_customer()
    response = add(customer_id, headers,
                   {"line1": "  5 New Street ", "suburb": " Newtown", "state": " NSW ", "postcode": "2042 "})
    assert response.status_code == 201, response.text
    assert response.json() == {"address_no": 1, **NEW_ADDRESS, "is_active": True}


def test_a_lower_case_state_is_the_databases_call():
    """Pydantic only trims and checks lengths; whether 'nsw' is a state is the au_state domain's decision."""
    customer_id, headers = new_customer()
    response = add(customer_id, headers, {**NEW_ADDRESS, "state": "nsw"})
    assert (response.status_code, response.json()["error"]["code"]) == (409, "au_state_valid"), response.text


def test_concurrent_adds_get_distinct_numbers():
    customer_id, _ = new_customer()
    owner = Actor("customer", customer_id, "Test User")

    def one_request():
        """What the API does for one POST: its own connection and one transaction (app/db.py)."""
        conn = connect()
        try:
            with conn, conn.cursor() as cur:
                return dict(add_address(cur, owner, customer_id, AddressCreate(**NEW_ADDRESS)))
        finally:
            conn.close()

    results = run_at_once(3, one_request)
    assert all(isinstance(result, dict) for result in results), results
    # The customer row lock made the three adds take turns, and each saw the one before it.
    assert sorted(result["address_no"] for result in results) == [1, 2, 3]


def test_retiring_an_address_keeps_it_and_retiring_it_again_changes_nothing():
    customer_id, headers = new_customer()
    add(customer_id, headers)
    url = f"{API}/customers/{customer_id}/addresses/1"
    first = client.patch(url, headers=headers, json=RETIRE)
    assert first.status_code == 200, first.text
    assert first.json() == {"address_no": 1, **NEW_ADDRESS, "is_active": False}
    again = client.patch(url, headers=headers, json=RETIRE)
    assert (again.status_code, again.json()) == (200, first.json())
    assert addresses_of(customer_id, headers) == [(1, False)]          # kept: past orders still point at it


def test_retiring_is_the_only_change_an_address_accepts():
    customer_id, headers = new_customer()
    add(customer_id, headers)
    url = f"{API}/customers/{customer_id}/addresses/1"
    for body in ({"line1": "6 Other Street"}, {"is_active": True}, {"is_active": False, "line1": "6 Other Street"}):
        assert error_of(client.patch(url, headers=headers, json=body)) == (422, "validation_error"), body
    assert addresses_of(customer_id, headers) == [(1, True)]


def test_a_new_address_must_be_well_formed():
    customer_id, headers = new_customer()
    # What a state or a postcode is, the database's domains decide (409, named after the domain's CHECK);
    # the API only checks the shape of the request (422).
    assert error_of(add(customer_id, headers, {**NEW_ADDRESS, "state": "XX"})) == (409, "au_state_valid")
    assert error_of(add(customer_id, headers, {**NEW_ADDRESS, "postcode": "ABCD"})) == (409, "au_postcode_format")
    assert error_of(add(customer_id, headers, {**NEW_ADDRESS, "postcode": "12"})) == (422, "validation_error")
    assert error_of(add(customer_id, headers, {**NEW_ADDRESS, "line1": "   "})) == (422, "validation_error")
    # The number is the server's to give, so a client can't send one.
    assert error_of(add(customer_id, headers, {**NEW_ADDRESS, "address_no": 7})) == (422, "validation_error")
    assert addresses_of(customer_id, headers) == []


def test_only_the_customer_changes_their_addresses():
    customer_id, headers = new_customer()
    add(customer_id, headers)
    url = f"{API}/customers/{customer_id}/addresses"
    _, stranger = new_customer()
    assert error_of(client.post(url, headers=stranger, json=NEW_ADDRESS)) == (403, "not_your_account")
    assert error_of(client.patch(f"{url}/1", headers=stranger, json=RETIRE)) == (403, "not_your_account")
    # Staff never change a customer's addresses for them — not even support, who can look the customer up.
    for staff_id in (AIDEN, NORA):
        assert error_of(client.post(url, headers=as_staff(staff_id), json=NEW_ADDRESS)) == (403, "customer_only")
        assert error_of(client.patch(f"{url}/1", headers=as_staff(staff_id), json=RETIRE)) == (403, "customer_only")
    assert addresses_of(customer_id, headers) == [(1, True)]


def test_a_missing_address_is_404_for_its_owner_and_403_for_anyone_else():
    customer_id, headers = new_customer()
    url = f"{API}/customers/{customer_id}/addresses/99"
    assert error_of(client.patch(url, headers=headers, json=RETIRE)) == (404, "not_found")
    _, stranger = new_customer()
    assert error_of(client.patch(url, headers=stranger, json=RETIRE)) == (403, "not_your_account")
    assert error_of(client.patch(url, headers=as_staff(NORA), json=RETIRE)) == (403, "customer_only")
```

Each test signs up a brand-new customer (a new email through `GET /me`), because these requests commit: a seeded customer's addresses (Alice's, Ben's) are never touched. The concurrency test calls the service the way `app/db.py` runs a request (own connection, one transaction), like `tests/test_transactions.py`.

In `tests/test_permissions.py`, add two body constants directly after the `PRODUCT = {"sku": "APL-AP4", …}` line:

```python
ADDRESS = {"line1": "1 Test St", "suburb": "Sydney", "state": "XX", "postcode": "2000"}   # au_state_valid
RETIRE = {"is_active": False}
```

and these three rows at the end of the `ENDPOINTS` list, just before its closing `]`:

```python
    ("POST", "/customers/{alice}/addresses", ADDRESS, {"alice"}),           # 409: XX is no state, nothing added
    ("POST", "/customers/999999/addresses", ADDRESS, set()),
    ("PATCH", "/customers/{alice}/addresses/999999", RETIRE, {"alice"}),    # 404: no such address
```

Both bodies are well-formed, so a guest gets through validation to the permission check (401, not 422), yet nothing changes: Alice's own POST is refused by the `au_state` domain (409) and her PATCH names an address she doesn't have (404). Only Alice changes Alice's addresses — Ben gets 403 `not_your_account`, every staff member 403 `customer_only`.

- [ ] **Step 4: `require_customer` and `require_own_account` in `app/auth.py`**

Insert after `require_customer_self` (before `def require_staff`):

```python
def require_customer(actor: Actor | None) -> Actor:
    """Shopping, and a customer's own account, are for signed-in customers: staff sign-ins can't do them."""
    actor = require_actor(actor)
    if actor.kind != "customer":
        raise ApiError(403, "customer_only", "Sign in as a customer to shop.")
    return actor


def require_own_account(actor: Actor | None, customer_id: int) -> Actor:
    """Changing a customer's own account (their addresses): that customer only. Support staff can look an
    account up (require_customer_self) but never change it for the customer."""
    actor = require_customer(actor)
    if actor.id != customer_id:
        raise ApiError(403, "not_your_account", "That account belongs to another customer.")
    return actor
```

Support staff may *read* a customer's record (`require_customer_self`), but only the customer changes it, so all staff, support included, get `customer_only` here. Task 7's checkout reuses `require_customer`.

- [ ] **Step 5: The request models in `app/schemas/customers.py`**

Replace the import lines at the top with:

```python
from datetime import datetime
from typing import Annotated, Literal

from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, StringConstraints
```

and append:

```python
class AddressCreate(BaseModel):
    """A new delivery address. It is never edited afterwards (BR16): add a new one, retire the old one.

    Shape only: each field is trimmed, then its length checked. Which states and postcodes exist is the
    database's call — the au_state and au_postcode domains answer 409 au_state_valid / au_postcode_format.
    """
    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")       # the address number is the server's to give

    line1: Annotated[str, StringConstraints(strip_whitespace=True, min_length=1, max_length=200)]
    suburb: Annotated[str, StringConstraints(strip_whitespace=True, min_length=1, max_length=100)]
    state: Annotated[str, StringConstraints(strip_whitespace=True, min_length=2, max_length=3)]
    postcode: Annotated[str, StringConstraints(strip_whitespace=True, min_length=4, max_length=4)]


class AddressRetire(BaseModel):
    """Retiring is the only change an address accepts, and it only goes one way."""
    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")

    is_active: Literal[False]
```

`StringConstraints` trims before it checks the length, so `"   "` is a 422 and `" NSW "` arrives as `"NSW"`. Nothing is upper-cased: whether `nsw`, `XX` or `ABCD` is acceptable is not decided here — the `au_state` and `au_postcode` domains answer with a 409 (Pydantic checks shape only).

- [ ] **Step 6: The service functions in `app/services/customers.py`**

Replace the docstring and import lines at the top with:

```python
"""Customers: the support team's directory, each customer's own record, and their delivery addresses."""
from app.auth import require_customer_self, require_own_account, require_support
from app.errors import NotFound
```

and append:

```python
def add_address(cur, actor, customer_id: int, body):
    """Add a delivery address under the customer's next address number (a weak entity: 1, 2, 3, … per customer).

    Two adds at once would both read the same max(address_no), so the customer row is locked first: the second
    add waits for the first to commit, and its INSERT — a new statement, so a new snapshot under READ
    COMMITTED — then sees the first one's address and takes the next number.
    """
    require_own_account(actor, customer_id)
    cur.execute("SELECT 1 FROM customer WHERE customer_id = %s FOR UPDATE", (customer_id,))
    cur.execute(
        """
        INSERT INTO customer_address (customer_id, address_no, line1, suburb, state, postcode)
        SELECT %s, COALESCE(max(address_no), 0) + 1, %s, %s, %s, %s
        FROM customer_address
        WHERE customer_id = %s
        RETURNING address_no, line1, suburb, state, postcode, is_active
        """,
        (customer_id, body.line1, body.suburb, body.state, body.postcode, customer_id),
    )
    return cur.fetchone()


def retire_address(cur, actor, customer_id: int, address_no: int):
    """Retire an address: it stays, because orders placed with it point at it (BR16), but no new order may use
    it (BR17). Retiring it again changes nothing and answers with the same row."""
    require_own_account(actor, customer_id)
    # `AND is_active`: an address already retired is left alone, not rewritten.
    cur.execute(
        """
        UPDATE customer_address SET is_active = FALSE
        WHERE customer_id = %s AND address_no = %s AND is_active
        RETURNING address_no, line1, suburb, state, postcode, is_active
        """,
        (customer_id, address_no),
    )
    retired = cur.fetchone()
    if retired is not None:
        return retired
    cur.execute(
        """
        SELECT address_no, line1, suburb, state, postcode, is_active
        FROM customer_address
        WHERE customer_id = %s AND address_no = %s
        """,
        (customer_id, address_no),
    )
    address = cur.fetchone()
    if address is None:
        raise NotFound("Address", address_no)
    return address                                  # already retired: the same answer, nothing changed
```

Why the lock: `address_no` is numbered per customer (`customer_address` is a weak entity), and two transactions reading `max(address_no) + 1` at once get the same number — the second INSERT would fail on `customer_address_pk`. `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on the parent customer row makes concurrent adds for one customer take turns; under READ COMMITTED each statement takes a new snapshot, so the waiting add's INSERT sees the first one's committed row and takes the next number. Adds for different customers never wait for each other. (Checked while writing this plan: with the lock line removed, `test_concurrent_adds_get_distinct_numbers` fails every time with a `customer_address_pk` UniqueViolation.) The lock also briefly queues a checkout by the same customer behind an address add, because the order INSERT's foreign key takes `FOR KEY SHARE` on the customer row; neither then waits on anything the other holds, so this can't deadlock.

`retire_address` updates only `AND is_active`: retiring again — or a second retire racing the first, which waits for the row lock, re-checks the condition and updates nothing — changes nothing and re-reads the row, so both answers are the same 200. BR16's trigger allows the `is_active` change.

- [ ] **Step 7: The routes, and readable messages for the two domains**

In `app/routers/customers.py`, change the schema import to:

```python
from app.schemas.customers import Address, AddressCreate, AddressRetire, CustomerDetail, CustomerSummary
```

and append:

```python
@router.post("/{customer_id}/addresses", response_model=Address, status_code=201)
def add_address(customer_id: int, body: AddressCreate, cur: DbCursor, actor: CurrentActor):
    """Add a delivery address; the server gives it the next number. Only the customer themselves."""
    return customers.add_address(cur, actor, customer_id, body)


@router.patch("/{customer_id}/addresses/{address_no}", response_model=Address)
def retire_address(customer_id: int, address_no: int, body: AddressRetire, cur: DbCursor, actor: CurrentActor):
    """Retire an address — `{"is_active": false}` is the only change accepted (BR16). Doing it twice is harmless."""
    return customers.retire_address(cur, actor, customer_id, address_no)
```

The handlers stay thin: the permission check is the service's first line. `body: AddressRetire` is there only so that anything other than `{"is_active": false}` is a 422.

In `app/errors.py`, add two entries at the end of `CONSTRAINT_MESSAGES`, after `"promotion_product_fk": …`:

```python
    "au_state_valid": "That isn't an Australian state or territory: use NSW, VIC, QLD, WA, SA, TAS, ACT or NT.",
    "au_postcode_format": "A postcode is four digits, e.g. 2000.",
```

Without them the client would see PostgreSQL's own text (`value for domain au_state violates check constraint "au_state_valid"`).

### Task 6: Order reads

**Files:**

- Modify: `tests/test_permissions.py` (`alice_order()`, four rows)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes:
  - Task 1/2 rules, through `tests/orders_support.py`: a charge only while the order is `placed` (`br20_charge_while_placed`); `placed → paid` only with ≥ 1 line and charges equal to `v_order_total.total` (`br20_paid_in_full`); lines only while `placed` (`br08_order_line_immutable`); history written only by the BR09 trigger.
  - Task 3 views: `v_order_summary(order_id, customer_id, placed_at, status, fulfilment_method, fulfilling_store_id, fulfilling_store_name, total, line_count, last_updated_at)`, `v_order_payment(order_id, charged, refunded, balance)` (all `numeric(10,2)`, one row per order), `v_order_timeline(order_id, seq, from_status, to_status, changed_at, changed_by_staff_id, changed_by_name)`; main's `v_product_current_price`, `v_order_total`, `order_status_transition`.
  - Task 5: `POST /api/v1/customers/{customer_id}/addresses` and `PATCH /api/v1/customers/{customer_id}/addresses/{address_no}` (tests give a new customer an address, and retire it).
  - main: `require_actor`, `require_customer_self` (401; 403 `not_your_account` / `support_only`), `NotFound`, `ERROR_RESPONSES`, `DbCursor`, `CurrentActor`, `Actor`; tests: `as_customer`, `as_staff`, `connect`, `unique_sku`.
- Produces:
  - `app.auth.require_order_reader(actor: Actor | None, order: dict | None) -> Actor`: 401 `not_signed_in`; support: any order; customer: own order, else 403 `not_your_order`; store staff: own store's order, else 403 `other_store` — the same 403 when `order` is None.
  - `app/schemas/orders.py`: `OrderLine(line_no, product_id, sku, name, quantity, unit_price: Decimal, line_total: Decimal)`, `DeliveryAddress(address_no, line1, suburb, state, postcode)`, `PaymentSummary(charged, refunded, balance: Decimal)`, `HistoryEntry(seq, from_status: str | None, to_status: str, changed_at: datetime, changed_by_staff_id: int | None, changed_by_name: str | None)`, `OrderSummary(order_id, customer_id, placed_at, status: str, fulfilment_method: str, fulfilling_store_id, fulfilling_store_name, total: Decimal, line_count, last_updated_at: datetime)`, `OrderDetail(OrderSummary + lines, delivery_address: DeliveryAddress | None, payment: PaymentSummary, history: list[HistoryEntry], allowed_next_statuses: list[str])`.
  - `app/services/orders.py`: module docstring (T1's locks and the FOR UPDATE-first rule); `SUMMARY_SELECT` (`psycopg2.sql.SQL`); `get_order(cur, actor, order_id: int) -> dict` (first SQL: `SELECT … FROM customer_order WHERE order_id = %s FOR SHARE`; 404 `not_found` for support only); `_lines(cur, order_id: int) -> list[dict]` (`OrderLine` rows in `line_no` order); `list_customer_orders(cur, actor, customer_id: int) -> list[dict]` (newest first; 404 `not_found` when support names a customer that doesn't exist).
  - `app/routers/orders.py`: `router = APIRouter(tags=["orders"], responses=ERROR_RESPONSES)` with `read_order` (`GET /orders/{order_id}` → `OrderDetail`) and `list_customer_orders` (`GET /customers/{customer_id}/orders` → `list[OrderSummary]`); included in `app/main.py` under `/api/v1`.
  - `tests/orders_support.py`: `customer_id(email: str) -> int` (creates the customer, with no addresses, if the email is new), `paid_order(email: str, items: list[tuple[str, int]], *, method: str = "click_collect", store_id: int = 1, address_no: int | None = None) -> int`, `move(order_id: int, *statuses: str, staff_id: int | None = None) -> None`.
  - `tests/test_permissions.py`: `alice_order()` and the `{alice_order}` path placeholder; four rows.

Ported from the read half of Yuhang's branch (`4a69838`: `get_order`, `_detail`, `_authorise` and `list_customer_orders` in `app/services/orders.py`; `app/schemas/orders.py`; `app/routers/orders.py`; `tests/test_order_reads.py`), changed per spec §4–§5: permission before existence (`require_order_reader` answers 403 where the branch answered 404, and runs before the 404); the order row is read `FOR SHARE` first; the figures come from the Task 3 views instead of ad-hoc joins and a list of payment rows; and `allowed_next_statuses` comes straight from the transition table, replacing the branch's per-actor `allowed_actions` (SP2) and `return_eligibility` (SP3). Checkout arrives in Task 7, so the tests build paid orders in SQL — the same rows T1 leaves behind. `web/src/lib/api/schema.d.ts` is regenerated in Task 8, not here.

- [ ] **Step 1: Test support — paid orders built in SQL**

Create `tests/orders_support.py`:

```python
"""Committed orders for the order tests, built in SQL exactly as a checkout (T1) leaves them.

Read tests don't go through POST /orders — checkout has its own tests — so they can't break when checkout
does. Each helper is one transaction on its own connection, committed, so the API (on other connections)
sees the result. Stock is not touched.
"""
from tests.support import connect


def customer_id(email: str) -> int:
    """The id of the customer with this email — created, with no addresses, the first time it is asked for
    (as a first sign-in with a new email would be)."""
    conn = connect()
    try:
        with conn, conn.cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute(
                """
                INSERT INTO customer (email, full_name) VALUES (%s, 'Order Test Customer')
                ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT br01_customer_email_unique DO NOTHING
                """,
                (email,),
            )
            cur.execute("SELECT customer_id FROM customer WHERE email = %s", (email,))
            return cur.fetchone()["customer_id"]
    finally:
        conn.close()


def paid_order(email: str, items: list[tuple[str, int]], *, method: str = "click_collect", store_id: int = 1,
               address_no: int | None = None) -> int:
    """A paid order for items = [(sku, quantity), ...]: lines at today's prices, numbered in product_id order;
    one charge of the total (none for a zero total); status placed → paid by the customer. Returns its id."""
    buyer = customer_id(email)
    conn = connect()
    try:
        with conn, conn.cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute(
                """
                INSERT INTO customer_order (customer_id, idempotency_key, fulfilment_method, fulfilling_store_id,
                                            delivery_address_no)
                VALUES (%s, gen_random_uuid(), %s, %s, %s)
                RETURNING order_id
                """,
                (buyer, method, store_id, address_no),
            )
            order_id = cur.fetchone()["order_id"]
            lines = []
            for sku, quantity in items:
                cur.execute("SELECT product_id FROM product WHERE sku = %s", (sku,))
                lines.append((cur.fetchone()["product_id"], quantity))
            for line_no, (product_id, quantity) in enumerate(sorted(lines), start=1):
                cur.execute(
                    """
                    INSERT INTO order_line (order_id, line_no, product_id, quantity, unit_price)
                    SELECT %s, %s, product_id, %s, current_price
                    FROM v_product_current_price
                    WHERE product_id = %s
                    """,
                    (order_id, line_no, quantity, product_id),
                )
            cur.execute(
                """
                INSERT INTO payment (order_id, kind, amount)
                SELECT order_id, 'charge', total FROM v_order_total WHERE order_id = %s AND total > 0
                """,
                (order_id,),
            )
            cur.execute("SELECT set_config('app.staff_id', '', true)")
            cur.execute("UPDATE customer_order SET status = 'paid' WHERE order_id = %s", (order_id,))
        return order_id
    finally:
        conn.close()


def move(order_id: int, *statuses: str, staff_id: int | None = None) -> None:
    """Commit the order's moves through statuses, made by staff_id (None = the customer or the system). BR09
    checks each move against the transition table and logs it."""
    conn = connect()
    try:
        with conn, conn.cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute("SELECT set_config('app.staff_id', %s, true)", ("" if staff_id is None else str(staff_id),))
            for status in statuses:
                cur.execute("UPDATE customer_order SET status = %s WHERE order_id = %s", (status, order_id))
    finally:
        conn.close()
```

`paid_order` writes in T1's order because the rules require it: lines while the order is `placed` (BR08), the charge while it is still `placed` (BR20), and only then `placed → paid`, which BR20 accepts because the charge equals the total; BR09 logs both history rows. `customer_id` creates a missing customer so a test can start from a brand-new email; signing in later with that email links to the same customer (`link_account` finds it by email).

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing tests**

Create `tests/test_order_reads.py`:

```python
"""Reading orders: GET /orders/{id} (one order in full) and GET /customers/{id}/orders (newest first).

Who may read which order, what a paid order shows, and that one read sees one moment. The orders are built
in SQL as a checkout leaves them (tests/orders_support.py), for customers who sign up inside each test, so
nothing here depends on what other tests committed.
"""
import uuid

import psycopg2
import pytest
from fastapi.testclient import TestClient

from app.auth import Actor
from app.main import app
from app.services.orders import get_order
from tests.auth_support import as_customer, as_staff
from tests.orders_support import customer_id, move, paid_order
from tests.support import connect, unique_sku

client = TestClient(app)
API = "/api/v1"
OLIVIA, AIDEN = 1, 14           # Sydney CBD (store 1): its manager, and a member of staff
ISLA = 16                       # Bondi Junction (store 2)
SAMUEL = 38                     # the warehouse (store 13)
NORA = 40                       # support
WAREHOUSE = 13
NEW_ADDRESS = {"line1": "5 New Street", "suburb": "Newtown", "state": "NSW", "postcode": "2042"}


def error_of(response):
    return response.status_code, response.json()["error"]["code"]


def new_email() -> str:
    return f"reader.{uuid.uuid4().hex[:10]}@example.com"


def read(order_id: int, headers: dict):
    return client.get(f"{API}/orders/{order_id}", headers=headers)


def committed(query: str, params=()):
    conn = connect()
    try:
        with conn, conn.cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute(query, params)
    finally:
        conn.close()


def with_an_address() -> tuple[str, int]:
    """A new customer who adds one delivery address: (email, address_no)."""
    email = new_email()
    response = client.post(f"{API}/customers/{customer_id(email)}/addresses", headers=as_customer(email),
                           json=NEW_ADDRESS)
    assert response.status_code == 201, response.text
    return email, response.json()["address_no"]


def a_product(price: str) -> str:
    """A new product selling at price (no promotion); returns its SKU."""
    sku = unique_sku()
    committed(
        """
        INSERT INTO product (sku, name, brand_id, category_id, base_price)
        SELECT %s, 'Order read test item', b.brand_id, c.category_id, %s
        FROM brand b, category c
        WHERE b.name = 'JBL' AND c.name = 'Speakers'
        """,
        (sku, price),
    )
    return sku


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- one order: who may read it

def test_a_customer_reads_their_own_order_with_money_as_two_decimal_strings():
    email = new_email()
    order_id = paid_order(email, [("APL-AP4", 2), ("JBL-FLIP7-B", 1)])      # on sale: 189.00 and 149.95
    response = read(order_id, as_customer(email))
    assert response.status_code == 200, response.text
    order = response.json()
    assert (order["order_id"], order["customer_id"], order["status"]) == (order_id, customer_id(email), "paid")
    assert (order["fulfilment_method"], order["fulfilling_store_id"], order["fulfilling_store_name"]) \
        == ("click_collect", 1, "Sydney CBD")
    assert (order["total"], order["line_count"], order["delivery_address"]) == ("527.95", 2, None)
    assert [line["line_no"] for line in order["lines"]] == [1, 2]
    lines = sorted((line["sku"], line["quantity"], line["unit_price"], line["line_total"]) for line in order["lines"])
    assert lines == [("APL-AP4", 2, "189.00", "378.00"), ("JBL-FLIP7-B", 1, "149.95", "149.95")]
    assert order["payment"] == {"charged": "527.95", "refunded": "0.00", "balance": "527.95"}
    assert [(entry["seq"], entry["from_status"], entry["to_status"], entry["changed_by_name"])
            for entry in order["history"]] == [(1, None, "placed", None), (2, "placed", "paid", None)]
    assert order["last_updated_at"] == order["history"][-1]["changed_at"]


def test_a_customer_gets_the_same_403_for_someone_elses_order_and_a_missing_one():
    owner = new_email()
    order_id = paid_order(owner, [("APL-AP4", 1)])
    stranger = as_customer(new_email())
    assert error_of(read(order_id, stranger)) == (403, "not_your_order")
    assert error_of(read(999999, stranger)) == (403, "not_your_order")      # so order ids can't be probed
    assert read(order_id, as_customer(owner)).status_code == 200


def test_store_staff_read_their_own_stores_orders_and_no_others():
    order_id = paid_order(new_email(), [("APL-AP4", 1)])                    # Click & Collect at Sydney CBD
    assert read(order_id, as_staff(AIDEN)).status_code == 200
    assert read(order_id, as_staff(OLIVIA)).status_code == 200
    assert error_of(read(order_id, as_staff(ISLA))) == (403, "other_store")
    assert error_of(read(999999, as_staff(ISLA))) == (403, "other_store")


def test_support_reads_any_order_and_gets_404_for_a_missing_one():
    email, address_no = with_an_address()
    order_id = paid_order(email, [("APL-AP4", 1)], method="delivery", store_id=WAREHOUSE, address_no=address_no)
    assert read(order_id, as_staff(NORA)).status_code == 200
    assert error_of(read(999999, as_staff(NORA))) == (404, "not_found")    # support may read every order
    assert error_of(read(order_id, as_staff(AIDEN))) == (403, "other_store")   # it ships from the warehouse


def test_a_guest_must_sign_in_to_read_orders():
    assert error_of(client.get(f"{API}/orders/1")) == (401, "not_signed_in")
    assert error_of(client.get(f"{API}/customers/1/orders")) == (401, "not_signed_in")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- what an order shows

def test_the_detail_keeps_the_sold_price_and_the_address_it_was_placed_with():
    email, address_no = with_an_address()
    sku = a_product("189.00")
    order_id = paid_order(email, [(sku, 1)], method="delivery", store_id=WAREHOUSE, address_no=address_no)
    # Afterwards the price goes up, and the customer retires the address.
    committed("UPDATE product SET base_price = 999.00 WHERE sku = %s", (sku,))
    headers = as_customer(email)
    retired = client.patch(f"{API}/customers/{customer_id(email)}/addresses/{address_no}", headers=headers,
                           json={"is_active": False})
    assert retired.status_code == 200, retired.text

    order = read(order_id, headers).json()
    assert (order["lines"][0]["unit_price"], order["total"]) == ("189.00", "189.00")    # the price it sold at (BR08)
    assert order["delivery_address"] == {"address_no": address_no, **NEW_ADDRESS}         # as placed (BR16)


def test_the_detail_lists_the_next_statuses_the_transition_table_allows():
    def next_statuses(order_id, headers=None):
        return read(order_id, headers or as_staff(NORA)).json()["allowed_next_statuses"]

    email = new_email()
    collect = paid_order(email, [("APL-AP4", 1)])
    assert next_statuses(collect) == ["picking", "cancelled"]                # the way forward first
    # The same list for every reader: it describes the order, not what the reader may do.
    assert next_statuses(collect, as_customer(email)) == next_statuses(collect, as_staff(AIDEN)) \
        == ["picking", "cancelled"]
    move(collect, "picking", "ready", staff_id=AIDEN)
    assert next_statuses(collect) == ["collected", "cancelled"]              # cancelled if never collected
    move(collect, "collected", staff_id=AIDEN)
    assert next_statuses(collect) == []

    email, address_no = with_an_address()
    delivery = paid_order(email, [("APL-AP4", 1)], method="delivery", store_id=WAREHOUSE, address_no=address_no)
    move(delivery, "picking", staff_id=SAMUEL)
    assert next_statuses(delivery) == ["shipped"]               # once a delivery is being picked, no cancelling


def start_picking(conn, order_id: int):
    """Aiden moves the order paid → picking on conn, giving up if he waits more than 200 ms for a lock."""
    with conn.cursor() as cur:
        cur.execute("SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '200ms'")
        cur.execute("SELECT set_config('app.staff_id', %s, true)", (str(AIDEN),))
        cur.execute("UPDATE customer_order SET status = 'picking' WHERE order_id = %s", (order_id,))


def test_reading_an_order_holds_off_a_status_change_until_the_read_ends():
    order_id = paid_order(new_email(), [("APL-AP4", 1)])
    support = Actor("staff", NORA, "Nora Fitzgerald", staff_type="support")
    reader, writer = connect(), connect()
    try:
        with reader.cursor() as cur:
            assert get_order(cur, support, order_id)["status"] == "paid"    # the read's transaction stays open
        # The read holds the order row FOR SHARE, so the status UPDATE waits for it — and gives up.
        with pytest.raises(psycopg2.errors.LockNotAvailable):
            start_picking(writer, order_id)
        writer.rollback()
        reader.commit()                                                     # the read ends: its lock goes
        start_picking(writer, order_id)
        writer.commit()
    finally:
        reader.close()
        writer.close()
    history = read(order_id, as_staff(NORA)).json()["history"]
    assert (history[-1]["from_status"], history[-1]["to_status"], history[-1]["changed_by_name"]) \
        == ("paid", "picking", "Aiden Chen")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- a customer's orders

def test_the_customer_lists_their_own_orders_newest_first():
    email = new_email()
    first = paid_order(email, [("APL-AP4", 1)])
    second = paid_order(email, [("JBL-FLIP7-B", 2)])
    response = client.get(f"{API}/customers/{customer_id(email)}/orders", headers=as_customer(email))
    assert response.status_code == 200, response.text
    assert [(order["order_id"], order["status"], order["total"], order["line_count"], order["fulfilling_store_name"])
            for order in response.json()] == [(second, "paid", "299.90", 1, "Sydney CBD"),
                                              (first, "paid", "189.00", 1, "Sydney CBD")]


def test_a_customer_cannot_list_another_customers_orders():
    owner = new_email()
    paid_order(owner, [("APL-AP4", 1)])
    url = f"{API}/customers/{customer_id(owner)}/orders"
    assert error_of(client.get(url, headers=as_customer(new_email()))) == (403, "not_your_account")


def test_support_lists_any_customers_orders_but_store_staff_cannot():
    email = new_email()
    order_id = paid_order(email, [("APL-AP4", 1)])                          # at Sydney CBD, Aiden's store
    url = f"{API}/customers/{customer_id(email)}/orders"
    response = client.get(url, headers=as_staff(NORA))
    assert response.status_code == 200, response.text
    assert [order["order_id"] for order in response.json()] == [order_id]
    assert error_of(client.get(url, headers=as_staff(AIDEN))) == (403, "support_only")
    assert error_of(client.get(f"{API}/customers/999999/orders", headers=as_staff(NORA))) == (404, "not_found")
```

`test_reading_an_order_holds_off_a_status_change_until_the_read_ends` keeps the reader's transaction open after `get_order`, so its `FOR SHARE` lock is still held; the writer's `SET LOCAL lock_timeout` turns "waits for the lock" into a `LockNotAvailable` (SQLSTATE 55P03) after 200 ms instead of a hung test. After the reader commits, the same move goes through at once.

- [ ] **Step 5: `require_order_reader` in `app/auth.py`**

Append at the end of the file, after `require_catalogue_writer`:

```python
def require_order_reader(actor: Actor | None, order: dict | None) -> Actor:
    """Reading one order: its customer, staff of the store fulfilling it, or support staff (any order).

    `order` is the order's row, or None when there is no such order. Customers and store staff get the same
    403 for a missing order as for someone else's, so they can't probe which order ids exist; only support,
    who may read every order, is then told 404 (by the service).
    """
    actor = require_actor(actor)
    if actor.is_support:
        return actor
    if actor.kind == "customer":
        if order is None or order["customer_id"] != actor.id:
            raise ApiError(403, "not_your_order", "That order isn't one of yours.")
        return actor
    if order is None or order["fulfilling_store_id"] != actor.store_id:
        raise ApiError(403, "other_store", "You can only read your own store's orders.")
    return actor
```

- [ ] **Step 6: The read models — `app/schemas/orders.py`**

Create `app/schemas/orders.py`:

```python
"""What an order looks like to its readers. Statuses and fulfilment methods are plain strings here: the
database's order_status and fulfilment domains decide which values exist."""
from datetime import datetime
from decimal import Decimal

from pydantic import BaseModel


class OrderLine(BaseModel):
    line_no: int
    product_id: int
    sku: str
    name: str
    quantity: int
    unit_price: Decimal             # the price it was sold at (BR08), not today's price
    line_total: Decimal


class DeliveryAddress(BaseModel):
    """The address the order was placed with — still shown after the customer retires it (BR16)."""
    address_no: int
    line1: str
    suburb: str
    state: str
    postcode: str


class PaymentSummary(BaseModel):
    """From v_order_payment: charged, refunded, and the balance the shop holds (charged − refunded)."""
    charged: Decimal
    refunded: Decimal
    balance: Decimal


class HistoryEntry(BaseModel):
    """One row of v_order_timeline (R11)."""
    seq: int
    from_status: str | None         # None on the first row: the order's creation
    to_status: str
    changed_at: datetime
    changed_by_staff_id: int | None  # None when the customer or the system made the change
    changed_by_name: str | None


class OrderSummary(BaseModel):
    """One row of v_order_summary."""
    order_id: int
    customer_id: int
    placed_at: datetime
    status: str
    fulfilment_method: str
    fulfilling_store_id: int        # ships from (delivery) or is collected at (Click & Collect)
    fulfilling_store_name: str
    total: Decimal
    line_count: int
    last_updated_at: datetime       # when the status last changed (its newest history row)


class OrderDetail(OrderSummary):
    lines: list[OrderLine]
    delivery_address: DeliveryAddress | None    # None for Click & Collect
    payment: PaymentSummary
    history: list[HistoryEntry]                 # oldest first
    # Where the transition table (BR09) lets the order go from its current status — the same for every
    # reader, and shown as text: the staff screens that move orders come with sub-project 2.
    allowed_next_statuses: list[str]
```

Field names equal the view columns, so service rows go straight into the models. Money is `Decimal`, which Pydantic sends as a string — `"0.00"`, not `"0"`, thanks to Task 3's `numeric(10,2)` casts. Task 7 adds `OrderLineIn` and `OrderCreate` to this file.

- [ ] **Step 7: The service — `app/services/orders.py`**

Create `app/services/orders.py`:

```python
"""Orders: checkout (transaction T1, POST /orders) and reading orders.

Locks (report §4). T1 takes no explicit row locks; its statements and the rule triggers take them:
  - FOR KEY SHARE on the customer, the store, the delivery address and each product: the foreign-key checks
    of the order and line INSERTs, so none of those rows can be deleted (or its key changed) meanwhile;
  - FOR SHARE on a Click & Collect store: the BR06 trigger, so the store can't stop being retail meanwhile;
  - FOR NO KEY UPDATE on the inventory rows, in ascending product_id order: two baskets lock the products
    they share in the same order, so they can't deadlock;
  - FOR UPDATE on the order row: the BR08 and charge triggers, so no line or charge can race placed → paid.

Reading: get_order first locks the order row FOR SHARE. Other readers go ahead, but a status change waits
until the read's transaction ends, so the header, lines, payments and timeline describe the same moment.

Rule for later services that change an order (status moves, cancellation, returns): lock the order
FOR UPDATE first and call get_order last. Taking FOR SHARE first and upgrading it later can deadlock: two
transactions would each hold the shared lock and wait for the other to let it go.
"""
from psycopg2 import sql

from app.auth import require_actor, require_customer_self, require_order_reader
from app.errors import NotFound

# The columns of OrderSummary. The view works out the store's name, the total, the line count and when the
# status last changed, so every list and detail shows the same numbers.
SUMMARY_SELECT = sql.SQL(
    """
    SELECT order_id, customer_id, placed_at, status, fulfilment_method, fulfilling_store_id,
           fulfilling_store_name, total, line_count, last_updated_at
    FROM v_order_summary
    """
)


def get_order(cur, actor, order_id: int) -> dict:
    """One order in full (OrderDetail): for its customer, the staff of the store fulfilling it, or support."""
    actor = require_actor(actor)                    # a guest is turned away before any row is locked
    cur.execute(
        """
        SELECT order_id, customer_id, status, fulfilment_method, fulfilling_store_id, delivery_address_no
        FROM customer_order
        WHERE order_id = %s
        FOR SHARE
        """,
        (order_id,),
    )
    order = cur.fetchone()
    require_order_reader(actor, order)              # 403 before 404: the order may not even exist
    if order is None:                               # only support gets here for a missing order
        raise NotFound("Order", order_id)

    cur.execute(SUMMARY_SELECT + sql.SQL("WHERE order_id = %s"), (order_id,))
    detail = cur.fetchone()
    detail["lines"] = _lines(cur, order_id)
    detail["delivery_address"] = None
    if order["delivery_address_no"] is not None:
        # The address as placed, even if retired since: addresses are never edited (BR16).
        cur.execute(
            """
            SELECT address_no, line1, suburb, state, postcode
            FROM customer_address
            WHERE customer_id = %s AND address_no = %s
            """,
            (order["customer_id"], order["delivery_address_no"]),
        )
        detail["delivery_address"] = cur.fetchone()
    cur.execute("SELECT charged, refunded, balance FROM v_order_payment WHERE order_id = %s", (order_id,))
    detail["payment"] = cur.fetchone()
    # By seq, not changed_at: a checkout's placed and paid rows share one transaction's now().
    cur.execute(
        """
        SELECT seq, from_status, to_status, changed_at, changed_by_staff_id, changed_by_name
        FROM v_order_timeline
        WHERE order_id = %s
        ORDER BY seq
        """,
        (order_id,),
    )
    detail["history"] = cur.fetchall()
    # BR09's transition table for this order's method and status. `to_status = 'cancelled'` is false for
    # every other move, and false sorts first: the way forward, then cancelling.
    cur.execute(
        """
        SELECT to_status
        FROM order_status_transition
        WHERE fulfilment_method = %s AND from_status = %s
        ORDER BY to_status = 'cancelled', to_status
        """,
        (order["fulfilment_method"], order["status"]),
    )
    detail["allowed_next_statuses"] = [row["to_status"] for row in cur.fetchall()]
    return detail


def _lines(cur, order_id: int) -> list[dict]:
    """The order's lines (OrderLine) in line order, at the prices they were sold at."""
    cur.execute(
        """
        SELECT l.line_no, l.product_id, p.sku, p.name, l.quantity, l.unit_price,
               l.quantity * l.unit_price AS line_total
        FROM order_line l
        JOIN product p ON p.product_id = l.product_id
        WHERE l.order_id = %s
        ORDER BY l.line_no
        """,
        (order_id,),
    )
    return cur.fetchall()


def list_customer_orders(cur, actor, customer_id: int) -> list[dict]:
    """A customer's orders, newest first (OrderSummary): for that customer, or support staff."""
    require_customer_self(actor, customer_id)
    cur.execute("SELECT 1 FROM customer WHERE customer_id = %s", (customer_id,))
    if cur.fetchone() is None:
        raise NotFound("Customer", customer_id)     # only support can ask about someone who doesn't exist
    cur.execute(SUMMARY_SELECT + sql.SQL("WHERE customer_id = %s ORDER BY placed_at DESC, order_id DESC"),
                (customer_id,))
    return cur.fetchall()
```

`get_order`, in order:
1. `require_actor` — a guest gets 401 before any row is read or locked.
2. `SELECT … FOR SHARE` on the order row — the shared (S) lock from the transactions lectures. Other readers carry on, but a status change (an UPDATE, which needs the row's exclusive lock) waits until this transaction ends, so the header, lines, payment and timeline read next all describe the same moment. (Checked while writing this plan: without `FOR SHARE`, `test_reading_an_order_holds_off_a_status_change_until_the_read_ends` fails with `DID NOT RAISE LockNotAvailable`.)
3. `require_order_reader(actor, order)` — the permission check runs on the row, or on `None`, before any 404: a customer or store staff member can't tell a missing order from someone else's.
4. Only then the 404, which only support can reach.

The module docstring records T1's locks for report §4 and the rule later sub-projects must follow: lock an order `FOR UPDATE` first, call `get_order` last. A transaction may take `FOR SHARE` on a row it already holds exclusively, so checkout (Task 7) can end with `get_order` safely. `_lines` is a separate function so that checkout's `price_changed` answer can list the lines the same way. `list_customer_orders` uses main's `require_customer_self` (the customer, or support; store staff get 403 `support_only`) and then, like `GET /customers/{id}`, answers 404 for a customer that doesn't exist (only support gets that far).

- [ ] **Step 8: The router, included in the app**

Create `app/routers/orders.py`:

```python
from fastapi import APIRouter

from app.auth import CurrentActor
from app.db import DbCursor
from app.errors import ERROR_RESPONSES
from app.schemas.orders import OrderDetail, OrderSummary
from app.services import orders

router = APIRouter(tags=["orders"], responses=ERROR_RESPONSES)


@router.get("/orders/{order_id}", response_model=OrderDetail)
def read_order(order_id: int, cur: DbCursor, actor: CurrentActor):
    """One order: lines, delivery address, payments, status timeline and the statuses it can move to next."""
    return orders.get_order(cur, actor, order_id)


@router.get("/customers/{customer_id}/orders", response_model=list[OrderSummary])
def list_customer_orders(customer_id: int, cur: DbCursor, actor: CurrentActor):
    """A customer's orders, newest first: the customer themselves, or support staff."""
    return orders.list_customer_orders(cur, actor, customer_id)
```

In `app/main.py`, change the router import to:

```python
from app.routers import customers, inventory, me, orders, products, promotions, reference
```

and the loop to:

```python
for module in (me, reference, inventory, products, promotions, customers, orders):
```

The orders router has no prefix, so `/customers/{customer_id}/orders` sits beside the customers router's `/customers/{customer_id}` without clashing. The handler names become the OpenAPI operation ids (`read_order`, `list_customer_orders`), which must stay unique.
