- [ ] **Step 1: Test helpers: `tests/checkout_support.py`**

Checkout commits, so the tests build committed rows of their own. The service-level helpers (`checkout_request`, `checkout_in`) run `orders.checkout` exactly as `get_cursor` does (one connection, one transaction). The concurrency tests need them to hold a transaction open or to bound a lock wait.

Create `tests/checkout_support.py`:

```python
"""Helpers for the checkout tests (T1, spec §3): committed products and customers, order bodies, and one
checkout run the way the API runs it. Ported from the branch's tests/shop.py without its demo-cookie
clients: API tests sign in with tests.auth_support.

Checkout commits, so each test makes its own products, and its own customer whenever it changes one.
"""
import threading
import uuid
from decimal import Decimal

from app.auth import Actor
from app.schemas.orders import OrderCreate
from app.services import orders
from tests.support import connect, unique_sku

SYDNEY_CBD = 1          # retail
BONDI = 2               # Bondi Junction, retail
WAREHOUSE = 13          # Eastern Creek DC: ships deliveries, can't be a Click & Collect store (BR06)


def committed(query: str, params=()) -> list:
    """Run one statement in its own committed transaction; return its rows ([] if it returns none)."""
    conn = connect()
    try:
        with conn, conn.cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute(query, params)
            return cur.fetchall() if cur.description else []
    finally:
        conn.close()


def a_product(price: str = "100.00", *, stock: int | None = None, store_id: int = SYDNEY_CBD,
              sale: str | None = None, active: bool = True) -> dict:
    """A committed product (a JBL speaker) at `price`: optionally stocked at one store, on a promotion that is
    running now (`sale`), or already retired. Returns {product_id, sku, price}, price being what it sells for."""
    conn = connect()
    try:
        with conn, conn.cursor() as cur:
            sku = unique_sku()
            cur.execute(
                """
                INSERT INTO product (sku, name, brand_id, category_id, base_price, is_active)
                SELECT %s, 'Checkout test item', b.brand_id, c.category_id, %s, %s
                FROM brand b, category c
                WHERE b.name = 'JBL' AND c.name = 'Speakers'
                RETURNING product_id
                """,
                (sku, price, active),
            )
            product_id = cur.fetchone()["product_id"]
            if stock is not None:
                cur.execute("INSERT INTO inventory (store_id, product_id, quantity) VALUES (%s, %s, %s)",
                            (store_id, product_id, stock))
            if sale is not None:
                cur.execute(
                    """
                    INSERT INTO promotion (product_id, sale_price, starts_at, ends_at)
                    VALUES (%s, %s, now() - interval '1 day', now() + interval '1 day')
                    """,
                    (product_id, sale),
                )
    finally:
        conn.close()
    return {"product_id": product_id, "sku": sku, "price": price if sale is None else sale}


def a_customer(addresses: int = 1) -> tuple[int, str]:
    """A committed customer with that many current addresses, numbered 1, 2, … Returns (customer_id, email):
    tests.auth_support.as_customer(email) signs in as them."""
    email = f"checkout.{uuid.uuid4().hex[:10]}@example.com"
    conn = connect()
    try:
        with conn, conn.cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute("INSERT INTO customer (email, full_name) VALUES (%s, 'Checkout Tester') RETURNING customer_id",
                        (email,))
            customer_id = cur.fetchone()["customer_id"]
            for address_no in range(1, addresses + 1):
                cur.execute(
                    """
                    INSERT INTO customer_address (customer_id, address_no, line1, suburb, state, postcode)
                    VALUES (%s, %s, %s, 'Sydney', 'NSW', '2000')
                    """,
                    (customer_id, address_no, f"{address_no} Test St"),
                )
    finally:
        conn.close()
    return customer_id, email


def order_body(items, *, method: str = "click_collect", store_id: int = SYDNEY_CBD,
               address_no: int | None = None, expected_total: str | None = None) -> dict:
    """A POST /orders body for items = [(product, quantity), …], reviewed at the products' current prices."""
    # Decimal, never float: 3 × 19.95 must be exactly 59.85, as the database computes it.
    total = sum((Decimal(product["price"]) * quantity for product, quantity in items), Decimal("0.00"))
    return {
        "fulfilment_method": method,
        "fulfilling_store_id": store_id,
        "delivery_address_no": address_no,
        "items": [{"product_id": product["product_id"], "quantity": quantity} for product, quantity in items],
        "expected_total": str(total) if expected_total is None else expected_total,
    }


def quantity_at(store_id: int, product_id: int) -> int:
    """The committed stock of a product at a store."""
    rows = committed("SELECT quantity FROM inventory WHERE store_id = %s AND product_id = %s", (store_id, product_id))
    return rows[0]["quantity"]


def orders_with_key(key) -> list[int]:
    """The committed orders placed with this Idempotency-Key, by any customer."""
    rows = committed("SELECT order_id FROM customer_order WHERE idempotency_key = %s ORDER BY order_id", (str(key),))
    return [row["order_id"] for row in rows]


def a_shopper() -> Actor:
    """A new committed customer, as the signed-in actor a service is given."""
    customer_id, _ = a_customer()
    return Actor("customer", customer_id, "Checkout Tester")


def checkout_request(actor, key, body: dict, *, lock_timeout: str | None = None) -> tuple[dict, bool]:
    """One checkout the way the API runs it: its own connection and one transaction, committed if it returns
    and rolled back if it raises (app/db.py). lock_timeout bounds how long it may wait for a row lock."""
    conn = connect()
    try:
        with conn:
            return checkout_in(conn, actor, key, body, lock_timeout=lock_timeout)
    finally:
        conn.close()


def checkout_in(conn, actor, key, body: dict, *, lock_timeout: str | None = None) -> tuple[dict, bool]:
    """A checkout inside the transaction open on conn, left uncommitted: the caller commits or rolls back."""
    with conn.cursor() as cur:
        if lock_timeout is not None:
            cur.execute("SELECT set_config('lock_timeout', %s, true)", (lock_timeout,))
        return orders.checkout(cur, actor, key, OrderCreate.model_validate(body))


def run_together(*works) -> list:
    """Run each work() in its own thread, all released at the same moment; return each result or exception.
    (tests.support.run_at_once runs one work n times; a race between two different requests needs this.)"""
    start = threading.Barrier(len(works))
    results = [None] * len(works)

    def worker(i):
        start.wait()
        try:
            results[i] = works[i]()
        except Exception as error:
            results[i] = error

    threads = [threading.Thread(target=worker, args=(i,)) for i in range(len(works))]
    for thread in threads:
        thread.start()
    for thread in threads:
        thread.join()
    return results
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the checkout tests: `tests/test_checkout.py`**

The tests use the shared seed rows only where they read them. Alice's active address 1 is used for the delivery happy path, and Ben's retired address 1 for the BR17 refusal. Every other test builds its own customer, so key-based assertions (`orders_with_key`) can't be confused by other files' orders.

Create `tests/test_checkout.py`:

```python
"""T1 checkout through the API (spec §3). One request is one transaction: the order, its lines at the
database's prices, the stock, the charge and the move to paid are committed together, or none of them is.

Checkout commits, so each test makes its own products (tests/checkout_support.py), and its own customer
whenever it changes one or counts its orders.
"""
import uuid

import pytest
from fastapi.testclient import TestClient

from app.main import app
from app.services import orders
from tests.auth_support import as_customer, as_staff
from tests.checkout_support import (BONDI, SYDNEY_CBD, WAREHOUSE, a_customer, a_product, a_shopper,
                                    checkout_request, committed, order_body, orders_with_key, quantity_at)
from tests.orders_support import customer_id, move

client = TestClient(app)
API = "/api/v1"
ALICE = "alice.morgan@example.com"      # address 1: 12 Harris St, Pyrmont NSW 2009
BEN = "ben.okafor@example.com"          # address 1 retired (he moved out), address 2 current


def place(headers: dict, body: dict, key=None):
    """POST /orders as the signed-in caller, with this Idempotency-Key (a new one if none is given)."""
    return client.post(f"{API}/orders", json=body, headers={**headers, "Idempotency-Key": str(key or uuid.uuid4())})


def error_of(response) -> tuple[int, str]:
    return response.status_code, response.json()["error"]["code"]


def payments_of(order_id: int) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:
    """The order's committed payment rows as (kind, amount)."""
    rows = committed("SELECT kind, amount FROM payment WHERE order_id = %s ORDER BY payment_id", (order_id,))
    return [(row["kind"], str(row["amount"])) for row in rows]


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- the two ways to get an order

def test_click_and_collect_writes_the_order_lines_stock_charge_and_history():
    customer, email = a_customer()
    plain = a_product("189.00", stock=5)
    on_sale = a_product("100.00", stock=5, sale="80.00")        # made second: the higher product_id
    response = place(as_customer(email), order_body([(on_sale, 1), (plain, 2)]))
    assert response.status_code == 201, response.text
    order = response.json()

    assert (order["customer_id"], order["status"], order["fulfilment_method"]) == (customer, "paid", "click_collect")
    assert (order["fulfilling_store_id"], order["fulfilling_store_name"]) == (SYDNEY_CBD, "Sydney CBD")
    assert order["delivery_address"] is None
    # Priced by v_product_current_price at checkout: the running promotion's 80.00, not the 100.00 base price.
    assert [(line["line_no"], line["product_id"], line["quantity"], line["unit_price"], line["line_total"])
            for line in order["lines"]] == [(1, plain["product_id"], 2, "189.00", "378.00"),
                                            (2, on_sale["product_id"], 1, "80.00", "80.00")]
    assert (order["total"], order["line_count"]) == ("458.00", 2)
    assert (quantity_at(SYDNEY_CBD, plain["product_id"]), quantity_at(SYDNEY_CBD, on_sale["product_id"])) == (3, 4)
    assert payments_of(order["order_id"]) == [("charge", "458.00")]
    assert order["payment"] == {"charged": "458.00", "refunded": "0.00", "balance": "458.00"}
    # Placed and paid in one transaction, by the customer: no staff member on either history row.
    assert [(h["seq"], h["from_status"], h["to_status"], h["changed_by_staff_id"], h["changed_by_name"])
            for h in order["history"]] == [(1, None, "placed", None, None), (2, "placed", "paid", None, None)]


def test_delivery_ships_from_the_warehouse_to_the_customers_address():
    product = a_product("99.95", stock=3, store_id=WAREHOUSE)
    body = order_body([(product, 1)], method="delivery", store_id=WAREHOUSE, address_no=1)
    response = place(as_customer(ALICE), body)
    assert response.status_code == 201, response.text
    order = response.json()

    assert (order["customer_id"], order["status"], order["fulfilment_method"]) == (customer_id(ALICE), "paid",
                                                                                  "delivery")
    assert (order["fulfilling_store_id"], order["fulfilling_store_name"]) == (WAREHOUSE, "Eastern Creek DC")
    assert order["delivery_address"] == {"address_no": 1, "line1": "12 Harris St", "suburb": "Pyrmont",
                                         "state": "NSW", "postcode": "2009"}
    assert [(line["line_no"], line["quantity"], line["unit_price"]) for line in order["lines"]] == [(1, 1, "99.95")]
    assert order["total"] == "99.95"
    assert quantity_at(WAREHOUSE, product["product_id"]) == 2
    assert payments_of(order["order_id"]) == [("charge", "99.95")]
    assert [(h["from_status"], h["to_status"], h["changed_by_staff_id"]) for h in order["history"]] == [
        (None, "placed", None), ("placed", "paid", None)]


def test_delivery_can_ship_from_a_retail_store_with_stock():
    """The web app pre-selects the warehouse for delivery, but the customer may ship from any store with stock."""
    _, email = a_customer()
    product = a_product("50.00", stock=2, store_id=SYDNEY_CBD)              # none at the warehouse
    response = place(as_customer(email),
                     order_body([(product, 1)], method="delivery", store_id=SYDNEY_CBD, address_no=1))
    assert response.status_code == 201, response.text
    order = response.json()
    assert (order["fulfilment_method"], order["fulfilling_store_id"], order["fulfilling_store_name"]) == (
        "delivery", SYDNEY_CBD, "Sydney CBD")
    assert quantity_at(SYDNEY_CBD, product["product_id"]) == 1
    assert committed("SELECT count(*) AS n FROM inventory WHERE store_id = %s AND product_id = %s",
                     (WAREHOUSE, product["product_id"]))[0]["n"] == 0
    # The fulfilling store's staff can read it; another store's cannot.
    assert client.get(f"{API}/orders/{order['order_id']}", headers=as_staff(14)).status_code == 200
    assert error_of(client.get(f"{API}/orders/{order['order_id']}", headers=as_staff(16))) == (403, "other_store")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- retries (BR10)

def test_a_key_retried_after_its_order_moved_on_still_replays_it():
    """A tab left open after a network error retries long after: the store has started picking the order. The
    retry still answers 200 with that order as it is now; nothing is placed, taken or charged again."""
    _, email = a_customer()
    product = a_product("30.00", stock=3)
    headers, body, key = as_customer(email), order_body([(product, 1)]), uuid.uuid4()
    first = place(headers, body, key)
    assert first.status_code == 201, first.text
    move(first.json()["order_id"], "picking", staff_id=14)
    again = place(headers, body, key)
    assert again.status_code == 200, again.text
    assert (again.json()["order_id"], again.json()["status"]) == (first.json()["order_id"], "picking")
    assert orders_with_key(key) == [first.json()["order_id"]]
    assert quantity_at(SYDNEY_CBD, product["product_id"]) == 2
    assert payments_of(first.json()["order_id"]) == [("charge", "30.00")]


def test_the_same_key_again_returns_the_first_order_and_changes_nothing():
    _, email = a_customer()
    product = a_product("189.00", stock=5)
    headers = as_customer(email)
    key = uuid.uuid4()

    first = place(headers, order_body([(product, 1)]), key)
    assert first.status_code == 201, first.text
    # Since then the price went up, and the customer changed the basket: the key still means the first order.
    committed("UPDATE product SET base_price = 249.00 WHERE product_id = %s", (product["product_id"],))
    again = place(headers, order_body([(product, 1)]), key)
    changed = place(headers, order_body([(product, 2)], expected_total="498.00"), key)
    assert (again.status_code, changed.status_code) == (200, 200)
    assert again.json() == first.json() and changed.json() == first.json()      # sold at 189.00 (BR08)
    assert orders_with_key(key) == [first.json()["order_id"]]
    assert payments_of(first.json()["order_id"]) == [("charge", "189.00")]
    assert quantity_at(SYDNEY_CBD, product["product_id"]) == 4                 # taken once


def test_a_retry_after_the_address_was_retired_replays_the_order():
    """The answer to a delivery checkout was lost, and the customer retired the address before retrying. The
    key is looked up before it is claimed, so the retry never reaches BR17's trigger: it gets its order."""
    customer, email = a_customer()
    product = a_product("99.95", stock=3, store_id=WAREHOUSE)
    headers = as_customer(email)
    body = order_body([(product, 1)], method="delivery", store_id=WAREHOUSE, address_no=1)
    key = uuid.uuid4()

    first = place(headers, body, key)
    assert first.status_code == 201, first.text
    committed("UPDATE customer_address SET is_active = FALSE WHERE customer_id = %s AND address_no = 1", (customer,))

    retry = place(headers, body, key)
    assert retry.status_code == 200, retry.text
    assert retry.json()["order_id"] == first.json()["order_id"]
    assert retry.json()["delivery_address"]["address_no"] == 1                  # where it was sent
    # A new attempt to that address is refused: the 200 above really was the replay.
    assert error_of(place(headers, body)) == (409, "br17_order_address_active")
    assert orders_with_key(key) == [first.json()["order_id"]]
    assert quantity_at(WAREHOUSE, product["product_id"]) == 2


def test_another_customers_key_never_replays_their_order():
    """BR10's key is unique per customer: someone else sending the same key places their own order."""
    product = a_product(stock=5)
    key = uuid.uuid4()
    placed = {}
    for _ in range(2):
        customer, email = a_customer()
        response = place(as_customer(email), order_body([(product, 1)]), key)
        assert response.status_code == 201, response.text
        assert response.json()["customer_id"] == customer
        placed[customer] = response.json()["order_id"]
    rows = committed("SELECT customer_id, order_id FROM customer_order WHERE idempotency_key = %s", (str(key),))
    assert {row["customer_id"]: row["order_id"] for row in rows} == placed
    assert len(set(placed.values())) == 2


def test_a_refused_checkout_does_not_use_up_its_key():
    """A refusal rolls the key's claim back with everything else, so the same reviewed basket can be sent
    again with the same key once the problem is gone (the web app keeps the key across retries)."""
    _, email = a_customer()
    product = a_product("50.00", stock=1)
    headers = as_customer(email)
    body = order_body([(product, 2)])
    key = uuid.uuid4()

    assert error_of(place(headers, body, key)) == (409, "out_of_stock")
    committed("UPDATE inventory SET quantity = 2 WHERE store_id = %s AND product_id = %s",
              (SYDNEY_CBD, product["product_id"]))
    retry = place(headers, body, key)
    assert retry.status_code == 201, retry.text
    assert orders_with_key(key) == [retry.json()["order_id"]]


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- the price the customer reviewed

def test_a_promotion_starting_after_review_is_a_price_change():
    """The customer reviewed $100.00, then a promotion started before they placed the order. A lower price is
    still a change: nothing is charged that wasn't reviewed, and the page gets the new lines to show."""
    _, email = a_customer()
    product = a_product("100.00", stock=5)
    headers = as_customer(email)
    assert client.get(f"{API}/products/{product['product_id']}").json()["current_price"] == "100.00"   # the review
    committed("""INSERT INTO promotion (product_id, sale_price, starts_at, ends_at)
                 VALUES (%s, 80.00, now(), now() + interval '1 day')""", (product["product_id"],))

    key = uuid.uuid4()
    response = place(headers, order_body([(product, 1)]), key)                  # still expects 100.00
    assert error_of(response) == (409, "price_changed")
    assert response.json()["error"]["detail"] == {
        "total": "80.00",
        "lines": [{"line_no": 1, "product_id": product["product_id"], "sku": product["sku"],
                   "name": "Checkout test item", "quantity": 1, "unit_price": "80.00", "line_total": "80.00"}],
    }
    assert orders_with_key(key) == []
    assert quantity_at(SYDNEY_CBD, product["product_id"]) == 5

    # Reviewed again at the new price, under a new key: placed at 80.00.
    again = place(headers, order_body([(product, 1)], expected_total="80.00"))
    assert again.status_code == 201, again.text
    assert again.json()["lines"][0]["unit_price"] == "80.00"


def test_a_zero_total_order_gets_no_charge_row():
    """A free item (a promotion at $0.00). A payment is always positive, so there is nothing to charge, and
    BR20 lets the order be paid because 0 charged = 0 due."""
    _, email = a_customer()
    product = a_product("10.00", stock=1, sale="0.00")
    response = place(as_customer(email), order_body([(product, 1)]))
    assert response.status_code == 201, response.text
    order = response.json()
    assert (order["status"], order["total"]) == ("paid", "0.00")
    assert order["payment"] == {"charged": "0.00", "refunded": "0.00", "balance": "0.00"}
    assert payments_of(order["order_id"]) == []
    assert quantity_at(SYDNEY_CBD, product["product_id"]) == 0


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- what the database refuses

def test_an_unknown_product_is_404_and_saves_nothing():
    _, email = a_customer()
    product = a_product(stock=5)
    body = order_body([(product, 1)])
    body["items"].append({"product_id": 999999, "quantity": 1})    # sorts last: the order and line 1 are in first
    key = uuid.uuid4()
    assert error_of(place(as_customer(email), body, key)) == (404, "not_found")
    assert orders_with_key(key) == []


def test_a_retired_product_cannot_be_ordered():
    _, email = a_customer()
    product = a_product(stock=5, active=False)
    key = uuid.uuid4()
    assert error_of(place(as_customer(email), order_body([(product, 1)]), key)) == (409, "br07_product_on_sale")
    assert orders_with_key(key) == []
    assert quantity_at(SYDNEY_CBD, product["product_id"]) == 5


def test_a_retired_address_cannot_be_delivered_to():
    """Ben's address 1 is retired in the seed (he moved): a new delivery there is refused by BR17's trigger."""
    product = a_product(stock=5, store_id=WAREHOUSE)
    key = uuid.uuid4()
    body = order_body([(product, 1)], method="delivery", store_id=WAREHOUSE, address_no=1)
    assert error_of(place(as_customer(BEN), body, key)) == (409, "br17_order_address_active")
    assert orders_with_key(key) == []


def test_the_address_must_fit_the_method_and_be_the_customers_own():
    _, email = a_customer()                                         # has address 1 only
    product = a_product(stock=5, store_id=WAREHOUSE)
    headers = as_customer(email)
    no_address = place(headers, order_body([(product, 1)], method="delivery", store_id=WAREHOUSE))
    assert error_of(no_address) == (409, "br06_order_address_matches_method")
    assert no_address.json()["error"]["message"] == \
        "A delivery order needs one of your addresses, and a Click & Collect order has none."
    collect_with_address = place(headers, order_body([(product, 1)], address_no=1))
    assert error_of(collect_with_address) == (409, "br06_order_address_matches_method")
    not_theirs = place(headers, order_body([(product, 1)], method="delivery", store_id=WAREHOUSE, address_no=99))
    assert error_of(not_theirs) == (409, "br17_order_address_is_customers")
    assert not_theirs.json()["error"]["message"] == "That address isn't one of yours."


def test_click_and_collect_is_refused_at_the_warehouse():
    _, email = a_customer()
    product = a_product(stock=5, store_id=WAREHOUSE)
    key = uuid.uuid4()
    body = order_body([(product, 1)], store_id=WAREHOUSE)
    assert error_of(place(as_customer(email), body, key)) == (409, "br06_collect_at_retail_store")
    assert orders_with_key(key) == []


def test_an_unknown_store_is_refused_by_its_foreign_key():
    _, email = a_customer()
    product = a_product(stock=5)
    body = order_body([(product, 1)], method="delivery", store_id=999999, address_no=1)
    response = place(as_customer(email), body)
    assert error_of(response) == (409, "customer_order_store_fk")
    assert response.json()["error"]["message"] == "That store doesn't exist."


def test_a_product_listed_twice_is_refused_by_br07():
    _, email = a_customer()
    product = a_product(stock=5)
    key = uuid.uuid4()
    response = place(as_customer(email), order_body([(product, 1), (product, 1)]), key)
    assert error_of(response) == (409, "br07_order_line_product_once")
    assert orders_with_key(key) == []
    assert quantity_at(SYDNEY_CBD, product["product_id"]) == 5


def test_a_quantity_below_one_is_refused_by_br07():
    """Quantity is only typed in the request model; BR07's CHECK refuses 0 and below, before any stock moves
    (a negative quantity must never add stock)."""
    _, email = a_customer()
    product = a_product(stock=5)
    headers = as_customer(email)
    for quantity in (0, -1):
        response = place(headers, order_body([(product, quantity)]))
        assert error_of(response) == (409, "br07_order_line_qty_positive")
        assert response.json()["error"]["message"] == "Each quantity must be at least 1."
    assert quantity_at(SYDNEY_CBD, product["product_id"]) == 5


def test_lines_are_numbered_in_product_id_order():
    _, email = a_customer()
    older = a_product("10.00", stock=5)
    newer = a_product("20.00", stock=5)                            # made second: the higher product_id
    response = place(as_customer(email), order_body([(newer, 1), (older, 1)]))
    assert response.status_code == 201, response.text
    assert [(line["line_no"], line["product_id"]) for line in response.json()["lines"]] == [
        (1, older["product_id"]), (2, newer["product_id"])]


def test_staff_cannot_check_out():
    """Shopping is for customers: store staff and support are refused before anything is read or written."""
    product = a_product(stock=5)
    for staff_id in (14, 40):                                       # Aiden (Sydney CBD), Nora (support)
        key = uuid.uuid4()
        assert error_of(place(as_staff(staff_id), order_body([(product, 1)]), key)) == (403, "customer_only")
        assert orders_with_key(key) == []
    assert quantity_at(SYDNEY_CBD, product["product_id"]) == 5


def test_out_of_stock_saves_nothing():
    """The second line is short. By then the order, both lines and the first line's stock were written; all
    of it is rolled back, and the page is told which product, how many were asked for and how many are left."""
    _, email = a_customer()
    plenty = a_product("10.00", stock=5)                            # made first: its stock is taken first
    short = a_product("20.00", stock=1)
    headers = as_customer(email)
    key = uuid.uuid4()

    response = place(headers, order_body([(plenty, 2), (short, 3)]), key)
    assert error_of(response) == (409, "out_of_stock")
    assert response.json()["error"]["detail"] == {"store_id": SYDNEY_CBD, "product_id": short["product_id"],
                                                  "sku": short["sku"], "requested": 3, "available": 1}
    assert orders_with_key(key) == []
    assert (quantity_at(SYDNEY_CBD, plenty["product_id"]), quantity_at(SYDNEY_CBD, short["product_id"])) == (5, 1)

    # A store that never stocked it has no inventory row at all: 0 left there.
    elsewhere = place(headers, order_body([(plenty, 1)], store_id=BONDI))
    assert error_of(elsewhere) == (409, "out_of_stock")
    assert elsewhere.json()["error"]["detail"]["available"] == 0


def test_a_failed_charge_rolls_back_everything(monkeypatch):
    """Plan §5's simulated payment failure. The charge is the last step before paid, so by then the order, its
    line, its first history row and the stock change are all written; the failure takes every one of them
    back, and the key is free for the retry."""
    actor = a_shopper()
    product = a_product("30.00", stock=4)
    body = order_body([(product, 1)])
    key = uuid.uuid4()
    seen = {}

    def declined(cur, order_id):
        cur.execute(
            """
            SELECT (SELECT count(*) FROM order_line WHERE order_id = %(id)s)           AS lines,
                   (SELECT count(*) FROM order_status_history WHERE order_id = %(id)s) AS history,
                   (SELECT quantity FROM inventory WHERE store_id = %(store)s AND product_id = %(product)s) AS stock
            """,
            {"id": order_id, "store": SYDNEY_CBD, "product": product["product_id"]},
        )
        seen.update(cur.fetchone(), order_id=order_id)
        raise RuntimeError("card declined")

    monkeypatch.setattr(orders, "_charge", declined)
    with pytest.raises(RuntimeError, match="card declined"):
        checkout_request(actor, key, body)
    assert (seen["lines"], seen["history"], seen["stock"]) == (1, 1, 3)     # written, inside the transaction
    left = committed(
        """
        SELECT (SELECT count(*) FROM customer_order       WHERE order_id = %(id)s) AS orders,
               (SELECT count(*) FROM order_line           WHERE order_id = %(id)s) AS lines,
               (SELECT count(*) FROM payment              WHERE order_id = %(id)s) AS payments,
               (SELECT count(*) FROM order_status_history WHERE order_id = %(id)s) AS history
        """,
        {"id": seen["order_id"]},
    )[0]
    assert left == {"orders": 0, "lines": 0, "payments": 0, "history": 0}
    assert quantity_at(SYDNEY_CBD, product["product_id"]) == 4

    monkeypatch.undo()                                              # this time the payment goes through
    detail, replayed = checkout_request(actor, key, body)
    assert (detail["status"], replayed) == ("paid", False)          # a new order: the key was free
    assert quantity_at(SYDNEY_CBD, product["product_id"]) == 3


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- the request's shape (422)

def test_a_request_of_the_wrong_shape_is_a_422():
    """Shape only: the price and the customer come from the server, so sending either is an error, not ignored."""
    _, email = a_customer()
    product = a_product(stock=5)
    headers = as_customer(email)
    body = order_body([(product, 1)])
    line = {"product_id": product["product_id"], "quantity": 1}
    for wrong in ({**body, "items": []},
                  {**body, "items": [{**line, "unit_price": "1.00"}]},
                  {**body, "customer_id": 1},
                  {**body, "expected_total": "100.001"},
                  {**body, "fulfilment_method": "drone"}):
        assert error_of(place(headers, wrong)) == (422, "validation_error"), wrong
    missing_key = client.post(f"{API}/orders", json=body, headers=headers)
    assert error_of(missing_key) == (422, "validation_error")
    not_a_uuid = client.post(f"{API}/orders", json=body, headers={**headers, "Idempotency-Key": "twice"})
    assert error_of(not_a_uuid) == (422, "validation_error")
    assert quantity_at(SYDNEY_CBD, product["product_id"]) == 5
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Append the concurrency tests to `tests/test_checkout_transactions.py`**

Task 2 created this file with the BR06 race test and the shared-lock guard. These tests drive the service directly, each "request" on its own connection, as `tests/test_transactions.py` does. That way a lock wait or a deadlock is decided by PostgreSQL, not by HTTP plumbing. Replace the file's import block (Task 2's) with exactly:

```python
import time
import uuid

import psycopg2
import pytest

from app.errors import ApiError
from tests import checkout_support as shop
from tests.support import connect, run_at_once
```

The helpers are imported as the module `shop`, so nothing here can clash with a helper Task 2 defined in the same file. Then append to the end of the file:

```python
# ---------------------------------------------------------------- T1 checkout under concurrency
# Each "request" is what POST /orders does: its own connection and one transaction (shop.checkout_request).
# These commit, so each test makes its own products and customers.

def test_concurrent_same_key_creates_one_order():
    """A double click: two requests with one key at the same moment. Whichever claims the key first places
    the order; the other waits on that claim in br10_order_idempotency_unique (or finds the order at its
    lookup) and answers with the same order instead of placing a second one."""
    product = shop.a_product("189.00", stock=5)
    actor = shop.a_shopper()
    body = shop.order_body([(product, 1)])
    key = uuid.uuid4()

    results = run_at_once(2, lambda: shop.checkout_request(actor, key, body, lock_timeout="10s"))
    assert all(isinstance(result, tuple) for result in results), results
    (first, first_replayed), (second, second_replayed) = results
    assert first["order_id"] == second["order_id"]
    assert sorted([first_replayed, second_replayed]) == [False, True]
    assert shop.orders_with_key(key) == [first["order_id"]]
    assert shop.committed("SELECT count(*) AS n FROM payment WHERE order_id = %s", (first["order_id"],))[0]["n"] == 1
    assert shop.quantity_at(shop.SYDNEY_CBD, product["product_id"]) == 4


def test_last_unit_has_one_buyer():
    """Two customers want the last unit. The first stock UPDATE locks the inventory row; the second waits,
    then re-checks `quantity >= 1` against the committed 0 and takes nothing: one order, one out_of_stock."""
    product = shop.a_product("499.00", stock=1)
    body = shop.order_body([(product, 1)])
    first, second = shop.a_shopper(), shop.a_shopper()

    results = shop.run_together(lambda: shop.checkout_request(first, uuid.uuid4(), body, lock_timeout="10s"),
                                lambda: shop.checkout_request(second, uuid.uuid4(), body, lock_timeout="10s"))
    sold = [result for result in results if isinstance(result, tuple)]
    refused = [result for result in results if isinstance(result, ApiError)]
    assert (len(sold), len(refused)) == (1, 1), results
    assert (refused[0].status, refused[0].code, refused[0].detail["available"]) == (409, "out_of_stock", 0)
    assert shop.quantity_at(shop.SYDNEY_CBD, product["product_id"]) == 0
    assert shop.committed("SELECT count(*) AS n FROM order_line WHERE product_id = %s",
                          (product["product_id"],))[0]["n"] == 1


def test_stock_is_locked_per_store_not_per_product():
    """A checkout locks the inventory rows of its own store, and nothing about the product itself. While a
    checkout of P at Bondi Junction is still open, one of P at Sydney CBD commits without waiting; another
    at Bondi Junction has to wait for the open one. (One customer throughout: nothing locks the customer.)"""
    product = shop.a_product("25.00", stock=5, store_id=shop.SYDNEY_CBD)
    shop.committed("INSERT INTO inventory (store_id, product_id, quantity) VALUES (%s, %s, 5)",
                   (shop.BONDI, product["product_id"]))
    actor = shop.a_shopper()
    at_bondi = shop.order_body([(product, 1)], store_id=shop.BONDI)
    at_sydney = shop.order_body([(product, 1)], store_id=shop.SYDNEY_CBD)

    held = connect()
    try:
        shop.checkout_in(held, actor, uuid.uuid4(), at_bondi)            # open: not committed yet
        # Another store's row: nothing to wait for, so the 5-second lock timeout is never reached.
        detail, _ = shop.checkout_request(actor, uuid.uuid4(), at_sydney, lock_timeout="5s")
        assert detail["status"] == "paid"
        # The same store's row: this one must wait for the open checkout, so its 1-second timeout runs out.
        with pytest.raises(psycopg2.errors.LockNotAvailable):
            shop.checkout_request(actor, uuid.uuid4(), at_bondi, lock_timeout="1s")
        held.commit()
    finally:
        held.close()
    assert shop.quantity_at(shop.SYDNEY_CBD, product["product_id"]) == 4
    assert shop.quantity_at(shop.BONDI, product["product_id"]) == 4


def test_baskets_in_opposite_product_order_do_not_deadlock():
    """[first, second] and [second, first] at the same moment, ten times over. Checkout takes stock in
    product_id order whatever order the basket lists, so neither can hold one row while it waits for the
    other's: no deadlock_detected, and both orders are placed every time."""
    first = shop.a_product("10.00", stock=50)
    second = shop.a_product("20.00", stock=50)
    forward = shop.order_body([(first, 1), (second, 1)])
    backward = shop.order_body([(second, 1), (first, 1)])
    one, other = shop.a_shopper(), shop.a_shopper()

    for _ in range(10):
        results = shop.run_together(lambda: shop.checkout_request(one, uuid.uuid4(), forward, lock_timeout="10s"),
                                    lambda: shop.checkout_request(other, uuid.uuid4(), backward, lock_timeout="10s"))
        assert all(isinstance(result, tuple) for result in results), results
    assert shop.quantity_at(shop.SYDNEY_CBD, first["product_id"]) == 30
    assert shop.quantity_at(shop.SYDNEY_CBD, second["product_id"]) == 30
```

The lock-timeout test cannot hang. `lock_timeout` only counts time spent waiting for a lock, so the Sydney CBD checkout never trips its 5-second timeout. Making Bondi Junction wait is what proves the inventory row lock. A regression to a product lock (the branch's `FOR UPDATE` on products) makes the Sydney CBD line raise `LockNotAvailable`.

```python
# ---------------------------------------------------------------- checkout (T1) requests

class OrderLineIn(BaseModel):
    """One line of the basket. There is no price: the server takes it from v_product_current_price (BR08).
    Quantity is only typed here; quantity > 0 is BR07's CHECK, answered as 409 br07_order_line_qty_positive."""

    quantity: int


class OrderCreate(BaseModel):

    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")

    fulfilling_store_id: int                        # ships from (delivery) or is collected at (Click & Collect)
    delivery_address_no: int | None = None          # delivery only: BR06's CHECK says so, not this model
```

- [ ] **Step 7: The transaction: `app/services/orders.py`**

The steps are those of spec §3, in that order. A few choices need explaining.

- **The key is looked up before it is claimed.** BEFORE INSERT triggers and CHECKs run before `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` skips a row. A retry that went straight to the claim would therefore meet BR17's trigger once the address had been retired, and get a 409 instead of its order. The plain SELECT answers a committed retry without touching any rule. `ON CONFLICT` still settles a double click that is in flight: the second INSERT waits for the first transaction, then skips the row.
- **There is no product lock.** The inventory row lock decides the last-unit race.
- **Stock is taken in `product_id` order.** That order is what keeps opposite baskets from deadlocking.
- **`get_order` is called last.** Its `FOR SHARE` comes after this transaction already holds the order `FOR UPDATE` through the BR08 and charge triggers, so the lock is never upgraded.
- **Nothing is cleaned up on errors.** Any exception ends the request's transaction in `get_cursor`, which rolls back everything, the key's claim included.

Replace the import lines Task 6 wrote:

```python

from app.auth import require_actor, require_customer_self, require_order_reader
from app.errors import NotFound
```

with

```python

from uuid import UUID

from psycopg2 import sql

from app.auth import require_actor, require_customer, require_customer_self, require_order_reader
from app.errors import ApiError, NotFound
from app.schemas.orders import OrderCreate
```

If Task 6's lines differ, keep them and add `Decimal`, `UUID`, `require_customer`, `ApiError` and `OrderCreate`. The module docstring Task 6 wrote already states T1's locks; leave it. Append to the end of the file:

```python
# ---------------------------------------------------------------- checkout (T1)

def checkout(cur, actor, key: UUID, body: OrderCreate) -> tuple[dict, bool]:
    """T1: place and pay for the basket the customer reviewed, in the request's one transaction (spec §3).

    Returns (order detail, replayed). replayed is True when this customer already placed an order with this
    key: that order is returned as it is now, and nothing is written. Any error rolls back everything this
    call wrote, its claim on the key included (app/db.py), so the same key can be sent again.
    """
    actor = require_customer(actor)

    # 1. Lookup. A retry whose first answer was lost gets that order back untouched, even if the address was
    #    retired or a price changed since. The body is not compared: a changed basket comes with a new key.
    placed = _order_with_key(cur, actor.id, key)
    if placed is not None:
        return get_order(cur, actor, placed), True

    # 2. Claim the key (BR10). If a request with the same key is still running (a double click), this INSERT
    #    waits for it: when it commits, DO NOTHING skips our row and we answer with its order; when it rolls
    #    back, ours goes in. The header's rules run here: BR06 (an address exactly when delivering; Click &
    #    Collect at a retail store), BR17 (the customer's own, current address) and the store's foreign key.
    cur.execute(
        """
        INSERT INTO customer_order (customer_id, idempotency_key, fulfilment_method, fulfilling_store_id,
                                    delivery_address_no)
        VALUES (%s, %s, %s, %s, %s)
        ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT br10_order_idempotency_unique DO NOTHING
        RETURNING order_id
        """,
        (actor.id, str(key), body.fulfilment_method, body.fulfilling_store_id, body.delivery_address_no),
    )
    claimed = cur.fetchone()
    if claimed is None:
        return get_order(cur, actor, _order_with_key(cur, actor.id, key)), True
    order_id = claimed["order_id"]

    # 3. Lines, numbered in product_id order, each priced by the view as of now: the client never sends a
    #    price (BR08). BR07 checks every line: still on sale, quantity above 0, each product once.
    items = sorted(body.items, key=lambda item: item.product_id)
    for line_no, item in enumerate(items, start=1):
        cur.execute(
            """
            INSERT INTO order_line (order_id, line_no, product_id, quantity, unit_price)
            SELECT %s, %s, product_id, %s, current_price
            FROM v_product_current_price
            WHERE product_id = %s
            RETURNING line_no
            """,
            (order_id, line_no, item.quantity, item.product_id),
        )
        if cur.fetchone() is None:                  # the view has no such product
            raise NotFound("Product", item.product_id)

    # 4. Price check: the customer pays exactly the total they reviewed, or nothing.
    cur.execute("SELECT total FROM v_order_total WHERE order_id = %s", (order_id,))
    total = cur.fetchone()["total"]
    if total != body.expected_total:
        raise _price_changed(cur, order_id, total)

    # 5. Stock, in the same product_id order, so two baskets that share products lock those rows in one order.
    for item in items:
        _take_stock(cur, body.fulfilling_store_id, item.product_id, item.quantity)

    # 6. Charge, then 7. the customer's own move to paid: no staff member in BR09's log (app.staff_id empty),
    #    and BR20 checks the charge equals the total. The COMMIT then runs the deferred br07_order_has_lines.
    _charge(cur, order_id)
    cur.execute("SELECT set_config('app.staff_id', '', true)")
    cur.execute("UPDATE customer_order SET status = 'paid' WHERE order_id = %s", (order_id,))
    return get_order(cur, actor, order_id), False


def _order_with_key(cur, customer_id: int, key: UUID) -> int | None:
    """The order this customer placed with this retry key, if any (BR10: at most one)."""
    cur.execute(
        "SELECT order_id FROM customer_order WHERE customer_id = %s AND idempotency_key = %s",
        (customer_id, str(key)),
    )
    row = cur.fetchone()
    return None if row is None else row["order_id"]


def _price_changed(cur, order_id: int, total: Decimal) -> ApiError:
    """The 409 for a total that moved since the review. It carries the lines as they would be bought now, so
    the page can show them for the customer to review again.

    Money goes in as strings, as everywhere else in the API: the error handler's jsonable_encoder would turn
    a Decimal into a float (189.0). The lines come from Task 6's _lines, so they read exactly like the order's.
    """
    lines = [{**line, "unit_price": str(line["unit_price"]), "line_total": str(line["line_total"])}
             for line in _lines(cur, order_id)]
    return ApiError(409, "price_changed", "The price changed while you were reviewing your order.",
                    {"total": str(total), "lines": lines})


def _take_stock(cur, store_id: int, product_id: int, quantity: int) -> None:
    """Take `quantity` units from the store's stock, only if it has that many (BR03 stays the backstop).

    A relative, conditional UPDATE. Its row lock makes a second checkout of the same row wait; under READ
    COMMITTED that one then re-evaluates its WHERE clause against the first one's committed row, so the last
    unit is sold once, with no lock on the product (spec §3, step 5).
    """
    cur.execute(
        """
        UPDATE inventory SET quantity = quantity - %s
        WHERE store_id = %s AND product_id = %s AND quantity >= %s
        RETURNING quantity
        """,
        (quantity, store_id, product_id, quantity),
    )
    if cur.fetchone() is not None:
        return
    # Too few, or never stocked here (no inventory row at all): say how many there are.
    cur.execute(
        """
        SELECT p.sku, COALESCE(i.quantity, 0) AS available
        FROM product p
        LEFT JOIN inventory i ON i.product_id = p.product_id AND i.store_id = %s
        WHERE p.product_id = %s
        """,
        (store_id, product_id),
    )
    stock = cur.fetchone()
    raise ApiError(409, "out_of_stock", f"Only {stock['available']} left of {stock['sku']} at this store.",
                   {"store_id": store_id, "product_id": product_id, "sku": stock["sku"],
                    "requested": quantity, "available": stock["available"]})


def _charge(cur, order_id: int) -> None:
    """Take payment of the order's total: plan §5's stand-in for a payment provider. A zero total is no
    charge at all (a payment is always positive), and BR20 still holds: 0 charged = 0 due.

    Its own function so a test can make the payment fail (test_a_failed_charge_rolls_back_everything).
    """
    cur.execute(
        """
        INSERT INTO payment (order_id, kind, amount)
        SELECT order_id, 'charge', total FROM v_order_total WHERE order_id = %s AND total > 0
        """,
        (order_id,),
    )
```

- [ ] **Step 8: The route: `app/routers/orders.py`**

Replace the lines Task 6 wrote at the top of the file:

```python
from fastapi import APIRouter

from app.auth import CurrentActor
from app.db import DbCursor
from app.errors import ERROR_RESPONSES
from app.schemas.orders import OrderDetail, OrderSummary
from app.services import orders

router = APIRouter(tags=["orders"], responses=ERROR_RESPONSES)
```

with

```python
from typing import Annotated
from uuid import UUID

from fastapi import APIRouter, Header, Response

from app.auth import CurrentActor
from app.db import DbCursor
from app.errors import ERROR_RESPONSES
from app.schemas.orders import OrderCreate, OrderDetail, OrderSummary
from app.services import orders

router = APIRouter(tags=["orders"], responses=ERROR_RESPONSES)

# The customer's retry token for one reviewed basket (BR10). Sending the same key again returns the order
# already placed with it (200) instead of placing a second one, so a lost answer can be retried safely.
IdempotencyKey = Annotated[UUID, Header(alias="Idempotency-Key")]
```

Then append to the end of the file:

```python
@router.post(
    "/orders",
    response_model=OrderDetail,
    status_code=201,
    responses={200: {"model": OrderDetail, "description": "A retry: the order already placed with this key"}},
)
def create_order(body: OrderCreate, response: Response, idempotency_key: IdempotencyKey, cur: DbCursor,
                 actor: CurrentActor):
    """Check out (T1): place and pay for the reviewed basket in one transaction. 201 for a new order; 200
    with the same order when this Idempotency-Key was already used by this customer."""
    order, replayed = orders.checkout(cur, actor, idempotency_key, body)
    if replayed:
        response.status_code = 200
    return order
```

The handler is a plain `def`, and `DbCursor` keeps `scope="function"`. The commit, which runs the deferred `br07_order_has_lines`, therefore happens before the response is sent. The `responses={200: …}` entry puts the replay into OpenAPI, so the generated client types both answers as `OrderDetail`.

- [ ] **Step 2: `web/src/lib/api/client.ts`: order and address types; which 409s are database rules**

The checkout service raises `price_changed` and `out_of_stock` itself (spec §6). Without this change the error box would head them "Rejected by the database".

Replace:

```ts
export type Me = Schemas["Me"];

/** Every API error has this shape; for database rules, `code` is the constraint name (brNN_…). */
```

with:

```ts
export type Me = Schemas["Me"];
export type Address = Schemas["Address"];
export type AddressCreate = Schemas["AddressCreate"];
export type OrderCreate = Schemas["OrderCreate"];
export type OrderSummary = Schemas["OrderSummary"];
export type OrderDetail = Schemas["OrderDetail"];

/** The 409s the application itself answers at checkout (T1): the price changed since the review, or the stock
 *  ran out. Every other 409 is the database refusing the request, with the constraint's name as the code. */
export const APP_CONFLICTS: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set(["price_changed", "out_of_stock"]);

/** Every API error has this shape; for database rules, `code` is the constraint name (brNN_…). */
```

and replace:

```ts
  /** True when the database itself refused the request (a constraint or trigger). */
  get isRule(): boolean {
    return this.status === 409;
  }
```

with:

```ts
  /** True when the database itself refused the request (a constraint or trigger) — not one of the
   *  application's own conflicts, so the error box doesn't call a changed price "rejected by the database". */
  get isRule(): boolean {
    return this.status === 409 && !APP_CONFLICTS.has(this.code);
  }
```

`web/vitest.config.mts`:

```ts
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";

import react from "@vitejs/plugin-react";
import { defineConfig } from "vitest/config";

// Unit tests for the web app's own logic: `npm run test:unit`. The config is an .mts file so Node always loads
// it as an ES module — Vite and its React plugin are ES modules only, and the branch's .ts config failed with
// ERR_REQUIRE_ESM on Node 20.9.
export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [react()],
  resolve: { alias: { "@": fileURLToPath(new URL("./src", import.meta.url)) } },
  test: {
    environment: "jsdom",            // the checkout attempt lives in sessionStorage
    include: ["src/**/*.test.{ts,tsx}"],
    setupFiles: ["./src/test/setup.ts"],
  },
});
```

`web/src/test/setup.ts`:

```ts
import "@testing-library/jest-dom/vitest";
import { cleanup } from "@testing-library/react";
import { afterEach } from "vitest";

// For component tests: one DOM per test. React Testing Library mounts into document.body, and a tree left
// behind would answer a later test's queries. (Vitest has no global afterEach, so RTL can't register this.)
afterEach(() => {
  cleanup();
});
```

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";

import { addItem, fromCents, lineTotalCents, MAX_QUANTITY, readCart, removeItem, setQuantity, toCents,
  writeCart } from "./cart";

describe("money in cents", () => {
  it("cart_totals_use_cents", () => {
    expect(toCents("19.95") * 3).toBe(5985);
    expect(toCents("0.10") + toCents("0.20")).toBe(30);
    expect(toCents("189")).toBe(18900);
    expect(toCents("0.05")).toBe(5);
    expect(fromCents(5985)).toBe("59.85");
    expect(lineTotalCents("19.95", 3)).toBe(5985);
    expect(lineTotalCents("0.00", 9)).toBe(0);
  });
});

describe("the stored cart", () => {
  it("cart_merges_duplicate_products", () => {
    const once = addItem([], 7, 2);
    const twice = addItem(once, 7, 1);
    expect(twice).toEqual([{ product_id: 7, quantity: 3 }]);
  });

  it("cart_rejects_invalid_quantities", () => {
    expect(addItem([], 1, 0)).toEqual([{ product_id: 1, quantity: 1 }]);       // clamped up to 1
    expect(addItem([], 1, -5)).toEqual([{ product_id: 1, quantity: 1 }]);
    expect(addItem([], 1, 1000)).toEqual([{ product_id: 1, quantity: MAX_QUANTITY }]);
    expect(addItem([], 1, 2.7)).toEqual([{ product_id: 1, quantity: 2 }]);
    // Setting zero or below removes the line rather than leaving an invalid one.
    expect(setQuantity([{ product_id: 1, quantity: 2 }], 1, 0)).toEqual([]);
    expect(removeItem([{ product_id: 1, quantity: 2 }], 1)).toEqual([]);
  });

  it("cart_recovers_from_corrupt_storage", () => {
    expect(readCart(null)).toEqual({ version: 1, items: [] });
    expect(readCart("not json at all")).toEqual({ version: 1, items: [] });
    expect(readCart(JSON.stringify({ version: 99, items: [{ product_id: 1, quantity: 1 }] })))
      .toEqual({ version: 1, items: [] });
    expect(readCart(JSON.stringify({ version: 1, items: "nope" }))).toEqual({ version: 1, items: [] });
    // Invalid rows are dropped, valid ones survive, and a duplicate product keeps its first line.
    const repaired = readCart(JSON.stringify({
      version: 1,
      items: [
        { product_id: 3, quantity: 2 },
        { product_id: 3, quantity: 5 },
        { product_id: -1, quantity: 1 },
        { product_id: 4, quantity: 0 },
        { product_id: 5, quantity: 1.5 },
        { product_id: "six", quantity: 1 },
        { product_id: 8, quantity: 4 },
      ],
    }));
    expect(repaired.items).toEqual([{ product_id: 3, quantity: 2 }, { product_id: 8, quantity: 4 }]);
  });

  it("round-trips what it writes", () => {
    const state = { version: 1 as const, items: [{ product_id: 2, quantity: 3 }] };
    expect(readCart(writeCart(state))).toEqual(state);
  });
});
```

`web/src/lib/checkout-attempt.test.ts`:

```ts
import { beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";

import { ATTEMPT_STORAGE_KEY, attemptFingerprint, clearAttempt, getOrCreateAttempt, keepsAttempt,
  pendingAttempt, twoDecimals } from "./checkout-attempt";

const body = {
  fulfilment_method: "click_collect" as const,
  fulfilling_store_id: 1,
  delivery_address_no: null,
  items: [{ product_id: 3, quantity: 1 }, { product_id: 2, quantity: 2 }],
  expected_total: "39.90",
};

describe("the reviewed checkout attempt", () => {
  beforeEach(() => {
    window.sessionStorage.clear();
    clearAttempt();                         // and the memory copy
  });

  it("same_attempt_reuses_key", () => {
    const first = getOrCreateAttempt(1, body);
    const again = getOrCreateAttempt(1, body);
    expect(again.key).toBe(first.key);
    // The same review with the lines in another order is still the same review.
    const reordered = getOrCreateAttempt(1, { ...body, items: [...body.items].reverse() });
    expect(reordered.key).toBe(first.key);
    expect(twoDecimals("19.9")).toBe("19.90");
  });

  it("changed_payload_uses_new_key", () => {
    const first = getOrCreateAttempt(1, body);
    expect(getOrCreateAttempt(1, { ...body, expected_total: "40.00" }).key).not.toBe(first.key);
    expect(getOrCreateAttempt(1, { ...body, fulfilment_method: "delivery", delivery_address_no: 1 }).key)
      .not.toBe(first.key);
    expect(getOrCreateAttempt(1, { ...body, items: [{ product_id: 3, quantity: 2 }] }).key).not.toBe(first.key);
  });

  it("customer_switch_discards_pending_attempt", () => {
    const first = getOrCreateAttempt(1, body);
    const other = getOrCreateAttempt(2, body);
    expect(other.key).not.toBe(first.key);
    expect(other.customer_id).toBe(2);
    expect(pendingAttempt()?.customer_id).toBe(2);      // the first customer's attempt is gone
    // And switching back is a new attempt too, not the old key resurrected.
    expect(getOrCreateAttempt(1, body).key).not.toBe(first.key);
  });

  it("unknown_outcome_preserves_body_and_key", () => {
    const attempt = getOrCreateAttempt(1, body);
    const kept = pendingAttempt();
    expect(kept).toEqual(attempt);
    expect(kept?.body).toEqual(body);
    clearAttempt();
    expect(pendingAttempt()).toBeUndefined();
  });

  it("ignores whatever else may be in that storage slot", () => {
    window.sessionStorage.setItem(ATTEMPT_STORAGE_KEY, "{not json}");
    expect(pendingAttempt()).toBeUndefined();
    window.sessionStorage.setItem(ATTEMPT_STORAGE_KEY, JSON.stringify({ hello: "world" }));
    expect(pendingAttempt()).toBeUndefined();
  });

  it("fingerprints the reviewed body, not the object identity", () => {
    expect(attemptFingerprint(body)).toBe(attemptFingerprint({ ...body }));
    expect(attemptFingerprint(body)).not.toBe(attemptFingerprint({ ...body, fulfilling_store_id: 2 }));
  });

  it("keeps the key unless the API refused the basket", () => {
    // Unknown outcome, or a sign-in problem that says nothing about an earlier try: keep key and body.
    for (const status of [0, 500, 503, 401, 403]) expect(keepsAttempt(status)).toBe(true);
    // The API refused this basket: nothing was placed, so the next review gets a new key.
    for (const status of [404, 409, 422]) expect(keepsAttempt(status)).toBe(false);
  });

  it("keeps the attempt in this page's memory when storage is blocked", () => {
    const blocked = () => {
      throw new DOMException("The operation is insecure.", "SecurityError");
    };
    const spies = [
      vi.spyOn(Storage.prototype, "getItem").mockImplementation(blocked),
      vi.spyOn(Storage.prototype, "setItem").mockImplementation(blocked),
      vi.spyOn(Storage.prototype, "removeItem").mockImplementation(blocked),
    ];
    try {
      const attempt = getOrCreateAttempt(1, body);
      // Within this page (client-side navigation included) the same review keeps its key.
      expect(pendingAttempt()).toEqual(attempt);
      expect(getOrCreateAttempt(1, body).key).toBe(attempt.key);
      clearAttempt();
      expect(pendingAttempt()).toBeUndefined();
    } finally {
      for (const spy of spies) spy.mockRestore();
    }
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 8: `web/src/lib/cart.ts`**

Ported from `4a69838:web/src/lib/cart.ts`. Only the doc comment's layout changed, and `CartState.version` is now written `typeof CART_VERSION`.

```ts
/**
 * The browser cart: product ids and quantities only.
 *
 * It reserves nothing and holds no prices — checkout always re-reads the current price from the database, and
 * the totals shown here are only what the customer reviewed. All arithmetic is in integer cents, so 19.95 × 3
 * is exactly 59.85 and never 59.849999999999994.
 */

export const CART_VERSION = 1;
export const CART_STORAGE_KEY = "jb.shop.cart";
export const MAX_QUANTITY = 99;

export type CartItem = { product_id: number; quantity: number };
export type CartState = { version: typeof CART_VERSION; items: CartItem[] };

export const EMPTY_CART: CartState = { version: CART_VERSION, items: [] };

function validQuantity(quantity: unknown): quantity is number {
  return typeof quantity === "number" && Number.isInteger(quantity) && quantity > 0 && quantity <= MAX_QUANTITY;
}

function validItem(item: unknown): item is CartItem {
  const candidate = item as CartItem | null;
  return candidate !== null && typeof candidate === "object"
    && Number.isInteger(candidate.product_id) && candidate.product_id > 0
    && validQuantity(candidate.quantity);
}

/** Read a cart from stored text. Anything unrecognised (older version, hand-edited, corrupt) is dropped. */
export function readCart(raw: string | null): CartState {
  if (!raw) return EMPTY_CART;
  try {
    const parsed = JSON.parse(raw) as Partial<CartState> | null;
    if (!parsed || parsed.version !== CART_VERSION || !Array.isArray(parsed.items)) return EMPTY_CART;
    const items = parsed.items.filter(validItem).map((item) => ({
      product_id: item.product_id,
      quantity: item.quantity,
    }));
    // A product listed twice would be ambiguous, so the first occurrence wins.
    const seen = new Set<number>();
    return {
      version: CART_VERSION,
      items: items.filter((item) => !seen.has(item.product_id) && seen.add(item.product_id)),
    };
  } catch {
    return EMPTY_CART;
  }
}

export function writeCart(state: CartState): string {
  return JSON.stringify(state);
}

function normalise(quantity: number): number {
  return Math.min(Math.max(Math.trunc(quantity), 1), MAX_QUANTITY);
}

/** Add a product, merging with a line that is already there rather than adding a second one. */
export function addItem(items: CartItem[], product_id: number, quantity = 1): CartItem[] {
  const existing = items.find((item) => item.product_id === product_id);
  if (!existing) return [...items, { product_id, quantity: normalise(quantity) }];
  return items.map((item) => item.product_id === product_id
    ? { ...item, quantity: normalise(item.quantity + quantity) }
    : item);
}

export function setQuantity(items: CartItem[], product_id: number, quantity: number): CartItem[] {
  if (quantity <= 0) return removeItem(items, product_id);
  return items.map((item) => item.product_id === product_id
    ? { ...item, quantity: normalise(quantity) }
    : item);
}

export function removeItem(items: CartItem[], product_id: number): CartItem[] {
  return items.filter((item) => item.product_id !== product_id);
}

/** Money as integer cents. `toCents("19.95")` is 1995, never 1994.9999999999998. */
export function toCents(decimal: string): number {
  const match = /^(-?)(\d*)(?:\.(\d*))?$/.exec(decimal.trim());
  if (!match) return 0;
  const [, sign, whole = "0", fraction = ""] = match;
  const cents = Number(whole) * 100 + Number((fraction + "00").slice(0, 2));
  return sign === "-" ? -cents : cents;
}

export function fromCents(cents: number): string {
  const sign = cents < 0 ? "-" : "";
  const absolute = Math.abs(cents);
  return `${sign}${Math.floor(absolute / 100)}.${String(absolute % 100).padStart(2, "0")}`;
}

/** One line's value, in cents, from the price the API reported. */
export function lineTotalCents(price: string, quantity: number): number {
  return toCents(price) * quantity;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 9: `web/src/lib/checkout-attempt.ts`**

Ported from `4a69838:web/src/lib/checkout-attempt.ts`, with the logic unchanged except for two additions: `keepsAttempt` (which failed sends keep their key), and a memory copy of the attempt for when sessionStorage is blocked (the branch's comment said the attempt "still works until the page reloads", but nothing held it). The comments now say why the browser keeps a fingerprint: the API replays a known key without comparing bodies (spec §3, step 1). The demo-session wording is gone. The caller passes the signed-in customer's id from `useShop()`.

```ts
/**
 * One reviewed checkout attempt, remembered across an uncertain network outcome.
 *
 * If the response never arrives, the customer must be able to send *exactly* the same request again: same
 * Idempotency-Key and same body, so the API either creates one order or returns the one it already created
 * (BR10). Changing the review (another address, store, quantity or total) is a different attempt and gets a
 * fresh key. The API replays whatever order a customer placed with a key, without comparing bodies, so it is
 * this fingerprint — kept only in the browser — that gives a changed review its own key.
 *
 * The attempt is kept per customer in sessionStorage (this tab only, so it survives a reload) and holds nothing
 * secret. When storage is blocked it is kept in memory, which lasts until the page reloads.
 */

export type CheckoutAttemptBody = {
  fulfilment_method: "delivery" | "click_collect";
  fulfilling_store_id: number;
  delivery_address_no?: number | null;
  items: { product_id: number; quantity: number }[];
  expected_total: string;
};

export type CheckoutAttempt = {
  customer_id: number;
  key: string;
  fingerprint: string;
  body: CheckoutAttemptBody;
};

export const ATTEMPT_STORAGE_KEY = "jb.shop.checkoutAttempt";

/** Money as two decimals, so "19.9" and "19.90" are the same review. */
export function twoDecimals(value: string | number): string {
  const [whole, fraction = ""] = String(value).trim().split(".");
  const negative = whole.startsWith("-");
  const digits = (negative ? whole.slice(1) : whole).replace(/\D/g, "") || "0";
  return `${negative ? "-" : ""}${digits}.${(fraction.replace(/\D/g, "") + "00").slice(0, 2)}`;
}

/** A stable summary of what the customer reviewed: same review, same fingerprint. */
export function attemptFingerprint(body: CheckoutAttemptBody): string {
  return JSON.stringify({
    fulfilment_method: body.fulfilment_method,
    fulfilling_store_id: body.fulfilling_store_id,
    delivery_address_no: body.delivery_address_no ?? null,
    expected_total: twoDecimals(body.expected_total),
    items: [...body.items]
      .sort((left, right) => left.product_id - right.product_id)
      .map((item) => ({ product_id: item.product_id, quantity: item.quantity })),
  });
}

/** A fresh Idempotency-Key: a random (version 4) UUID — the API refuses a key that isn't a UUID. */
export function newAttemptKey(): string {
  if (typeof crypto !== "undefined" && "randomUUID" in crypto) return crypto.randomUUID();
  // crypto.randomUUID needs a secure context (https, localhost or 127.0.0.1); elsewhere, build one by hand.
  return "10000000-1000-4000-8000-100000000000".replace(/[018]/g, (digit) =>
    (Number(digit) ^ (Math.random() * 16 >> Number(digit) / 4)).toString(16));
}

/** The attempt held in memory: the only copy when sessionStorage is blocked. */
let remembered: CheckoutAttempt | undefined;

export function pendingAttempt(): CheckoutAttempt | undefined {
  let raw: string | null;
  try {
    raw = window.sessionStorage.getItem(ATTEMPT_STORAGE_KEY);
  } catch {
    return remembered;                      // storage blocked: this page's memory
  }
  if (!raw) return undefined;
  try {
    const parsed = JSON.parse(raw) as CheckoutAttempt | null;
    return parsed && typeof parsed.key === "string" && typeof parsed.customer_id === "number"
      && typeof parsed.fingerprint === "string" && typeof parsed.body === "object" ? parsed : undefined;
  } catch {
    return undefined;                       // something else wrote this key
  }
}

/** Keep the attempt for a retry: in memory, and in sessionStorage so it survives a reload of this tab. */
function remember(attempt: CheckoutAttempt): void {
  remembered = attempt;
  try {
    window.sessionStorage.setItem(ATTEMPT_STORAGE_KEY, JSON.stringify(attempt));
  } catch {
    // Storage blocked: the memory copy lasts until the page reloads. After a reload the same review gets a new
    // key, so a response lost just before it could then end in a second order.
  }
}

/**
 * The attempt to send. A stored attempt is reused only when it is for this customer *and* this exact review;
 * anything else — including someone else signing in on this browser — starts a new attempt with a new key,
 * which replaces the stored one. So the checkout page calls this only when none of this customer's tries is
 * waiting for an answer: replacing a waiting try would lose the key that may have placed an order.
 */
export function getOrCreateAttempt(customerId: number, body: CheckoutAttemptBody): CheckoutAttempt {
  const fingerprint = attemptFingerprint(body);
  const stored = pendingAttempt();
  if (stored && stored.customer_id === customerId && stored.fingerprint === fingerprint) return stored;
  const attempt: CheckoutAttempt = { customer_id: customerId, key: newAttemptKey(), fingerprint, body };
  remember(attempt);
  return attempt;
}

/** The attempt got its answer (its order, or a refusal), or the customer started a new order instead. */
export function clearAttempt(): void {
  remembered = undefined;
  try {
    window.sessionStorage.removeItem(ATTEMPT_STORAGE_KEY);
  } catch {
    // storage blocked: nothing stored there
  }
}

/**
 * After a send that failed, keep the pending attempt (its key and body) for "Try again"? Yes when the outcome is
 * unknown — no answer (0), a server error (≥ 500), or a sign-in problem (401/403) that says nothing about whether
 * an earlier try with this key went through. No when the API refused the basket (404/409/422): nothing was
 * placed, so the next review gets a new key.
 */
export function keepsAttempt(status: number): boolean {
  return status === 0 || status >= 500 || status === 401 || status === 403;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 11: `web/src/lib/browser-storage.ts`, imported by the shop context**

This is the storage code moved out of `web/src/components/shop/context.tsx` unchanged, except that `subscribe` is now exported as `subscribeStored` so the cart can use it too.

```ts
"use client";

import { useSyncExternalStore } from "react";

/**
 * Small per-browser values — "My store", the cart — kept in localStorage and read through useSyncExternalStore:
 * the server render and hydration see nothing stored, then the browser's value appears, with no
 * effect-and-setState dance and no hydration mismatch. If storage is blocked (a private window), a value still
 * works in memory until the page reloads.
 */

const listeners = new Set<() => void>();
const memory = new Map<string, string | null>();

/** For useSyncExternalStore: `listener` runs after every write here, and when another tab writes. */
export function subscribeStored(listener: () => void) {
  listeners.add(listener);
  window.addEventListener("storage", listener);
  return () => {
    listeners.delete(listener);
    window.removeEventListener("storage", listener);
  };
}

export function readStored(key: string): string | null {
  try {
    return window.localStorage.getItem(key);
  } catch {
    return memory.get(key) ?? null;
  }
}

export function writeStored(key: string, value: string | null) {
  memory.set(key, value);
  try {
    if (value === null) window.localStorage.removeItem(key);
    else window.localStorage.setItem(key, value);
  } catch {
    // storage blocked: the in-memory value above still applies
  }
  listeners.forEach((listener) => listener());
}

/** A whole number stored under `key`, or undefined when nothing valid is stored. */
export function useStoredId(key: string): [number | undefined, (id: number | undefined) => void] {
  const raw = useSyncExternalStore(subscribeStored, () => readStored(key), () => null);
  const id = raw === null ? NaN : Number(raw);
  return [Number.isInteger(id) ? id : undefined, (next) => writeStored(key, next === undefined ? null : String(next))];
}
```

- [ ] **Step 12: `web/src/lib/cart-context.tsx`**

Adapted from `4a69838:web/src/lib/cart-context.tsx`. The API and the `useSyncExternalStore` approach are the same, with two changes:
- It reads and writes through `lib/browser-storage.ts` instead of its own copy of the localStorage and memory code.
- Each change starts from the stored cart, not from the last render's `items`, so two changes in one event both count (e.g. `addItem` then `removeSubmitted`).

The demo-session wording is gone. The cart holds nothing about who is signed in, so it survives Clerk sign-in and sign-out (spec §6).

```tsx
"use client";

import { createContext, type ReactNode, useContext, useMemo, useSyncExternalStore } from "react";

import { readStored, subscribeStored, writeStored } from "./browser-storage";
import {
  addItem as addToItems, CART_STORAGE_KEY, CART_VERSION, type CartItem, type CartState, EMPTY_CART, readCart,
  removeItem as removeFromItems, setQuantity as setQuantityIn, writeCart,
} from "./cart";

/**
 * The cart, kept in this browser (lib/browser-storage.ts). It holds no prices and nothing about who is signed
 * in, so it is kept across signing in and out: a guest fills a cart, then signs in at checkout.
 */

// useSyncExternalStore needs the same object back while the stored text is unchanged.
let cached: { raw: string | null; state: CartState } = { raw: null, state: EMPTY_CART };

function getSnapshot(): CartState {
  const raw = readStored(CART_STORAGE_KEY);
  if (raw !== cached.raw) cached = { raw, state: readCart(raw) };
  return cached.state;
}

const getServerSnapshot = () => EMPTY_CART;

// Each change starts from the stored cart, not from the last render, so two changes in one click both count.
function change(update: (items: CartItem[]) => CartItem[]) {
  writeStored(CART_STORAGE_KEY, writeCart({ version: CART_VERSION, items: update(getSnapshot().items) }));
}

const actions = {
  addItem: (product_id: number, quantity = 1) => change((items) => addToItems(items, product_id, quantity)),
  setQuantity: (product_id: number, quantity: number) =>
    change((items) => setQuantityIn(items, product_id, quantity)),
  removeItem: (product_id: number) => change((items) => removeFromItems(items, product_id)),
  /** After a checkout, take out only what was submitted: anything added meanwhile stays. */
  removeSubmitted: (submitted: CartItem[]) => change((items) => submitted.reduce(
    (remaining, sent) => setQuantityIn(remaining, sent.product_id,
      (remaining.find((line) => line.product_id === sent.product_id)?.quantity ?? 0) - sent.quantity),
    items)),
  clear: () => change(() => []),
};

type CartValue = typeof actions & {
  items: CartItem[];
  /** False during the server render and the hydration render, when the stored cart can't be read yet. */
  ready: boolean;
  /** Units in the cart, for the header. */
  count: number;
};

const CartContext = createContext<CartValue | null>(null);

export function useCart(): CartValue {
  const value = useContext(CartContext);
  if (!value) throw new Error("useCart() must be used inside <CartProvider>");
  return value;
}

export function CartProvider({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
  const { items } = useSyncExternalStore(subscribeStored, getSnapshot, getServerSnapshot);
  const ready = useSyncExternalStore(subscribeStored, () => true, () => false);
  const value = useMemo<CartValue>(() => ({
    ...actions,
    items,
    ready,
    count: items.reduce((total, item) => total + item.quantity, 0),
  }), [items, ready]);
  return <CartContext value={value}>{children}</CartContext>;
}
```

`web/src/app/(shop)/layout.tsx`, in full. `CartProvider` sits inside `ShopProvider` because the header uses both:

```tsx
import Link from "next/link";
import type { ReactNode } from "react";

import { ShopProvider } from "@/components/shop/context";
import { ShopHeader } from "@/components/shop/header";
import { CartProvider } from "@/lib/cart-context";

export default function ShopLayout({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
  return (
    <ShopProvider>
      <CartProvider>
        <div className="flex min-h-screen flex-col">
          <ShopHeader />
          <main className="mx-auto w-full max-w-7xl flex-1 px-4 py-6 md:py-8">{children}</main>
          <footer className="border-t border-neutral-200 bg-white">
            <div className="mx-auto flex max-w-7xl flex-wrap items-center justify-between gap-2 px-4 py-4 text-xs
              text-neutral-500">
              <span>COMP9120 prototype modelled on JB Hi-Fi — not the real store. Payments are simulated.</span>
              <Link href="/admin" className="font-medium text-neutral-700 hover:underline">Staff: store admin →</Link>
            </div>
          </footer>
        </div>
      </CartProvider>
    </ShopProvider>
  );
}
```
